python3 tools/gray_globe_distant_columns.py 10 --agda /tmp/GrayDistantColumns.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayDistantColumns.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 9

**Every cell of a globe pair can be recovered from its collapse to a smaller globe pair by one signed substitution and at most one final change of a stationary variable.** This gives a different sufficient condition for Q2. Its inputs are substitutions on *generator declarations* in one column. Given those inputs, the construction supplies the terms for all composite cells, including the two distant orders in Response 7. The first nontrivial threshold gives an unconditional extension of an old signed section across the whole subpresentation omitting bidegree $(m-1,2)$.

### The threshold maps

Write $m=p+1$ with $p\ge1$, $X=D^p\otimes D^n$ and $Y=D^m\otimes D^n$. Collapse $\pi:Y\to X$ kills first rank $m$ and forgets the first tag at rank $p$. For $0\le t\le n$ and $e\in\{0,1\}$ define $F_t^e:X\to Y$ as the identity below first rank $p$. On $A_p B_j^y$ set $s=y\mathbin\oplus(p\bmod2)$ for $j<t$, and $s=e$ otherwise. The first rule only encounters bit tags. Set
\[
 F_t^e(A_p B_j^y)= A_p^{s}B_j^y
    +[1\le j\le t]\, A_m B_{j-1}^{(1-s)\oplus(p\bmod2)} .
\]
In particular $F_0^e$ is the ordinary signed inclusion. To verify the differential, let $P(B_j^y)=s B_j^y$ and let $H=(-1)^p(P\partial-\partial P)$ on the unaugmented chains of the right globe. Then the displayed image is $A_p^- B+(A_p^+-A_p^-)P(B)+A_m H(B)$. Expanding the Leibniz differential and using $\partial H+H\partial=0$ proves the claim. Both maps preserve augmentation and positive chains, and $\pi F_t^e=1$. Further, $F_t^1-F_t^0=\partial K+K\partial$, where $K(A_pB_j^y)=[j\ge t]A_mB_j^y$ and $K$ vanishes at smaller first rank. This follows by subtracting the two formulas for $P,H$.

### Reconstructing an arbitrary cell

For a cell $w$ of $Y$, put $c=\pi w$. Let $u$ and $v$ be the largest transverse ranks occurring anywhere in its tableau at first ranks $m$ and $p$, respectively, with value $-1$ for an empty column. Downward closure gives $u\le v$.

- If $v=-1$, then $w=c$.
- If $u<v$, there is a unique $e$ such that $w=F_{u+1}^e(c)$. This includes the ordinary signed copies when $u=-1$.
- If $0\le u=v$, put $d=p+u$. The degree-$d$ rows of $c$ contain the same stationary label $S=A_pB_u^y$. Put $r=A_mB_u^y$ and let $z$ be the terminal $d$-pole of $c$ (or $c$ itself in dimension $d$). There is an elementary $(d+1)$-cell $R(z)$ with top chain $r$ and faces $F_u^0(z),F_u^1(z)$. Then $w=F_u^0(c)*_d R(z)$ with strict identities understood. In dimension $d$ of $c$ this is just $R(c)$.

Here is a proof of the tag assertions and the reconstruction. Every signed row of a globe pair has at most one label of each bidegree and its two sides have the same bidegrees. The projection already specifies the whole tableau except for the first tags at $p$ and the labels at $m$. At bidegree $(p,j)$ consider the differential from the next row. Its only possible contributors are $(p,j+1)$ and $(m,j)$. If both occur, their two directed edges in the square of tag pairs must cancel at one vertex, since the resulting difference has just one positive and one negative vertex. They therefore trace a path from $(0,p\bmod2)$ to $(1,1\oplus(p\bmod2))$. If $x$ is the first tag of $(p,j+1)$, the transverse tag of $(m,j)$ is $(1-x)\oplus(p\bmod2)$ on that same side of the next row. If only $(p,j+1)$ occurs, its first tag is inherited by both lower sides. If only $(m,j)$ occurs, both lower sides inherit its transverse tag and their first tags are $0,1$. If neither occurs, the two sides have identical labels at $(p,j)$.

In the second case of the classification this fixes a common first tag $e$ from $v$ down through $u+1$. Below that threshold the two-edge rule gives exactly $F_{u+1}^e$. In the third case the one-edge rule at $j=u$ fixes $y$ and gives first tags $0,1$. The lower rows on each side of degree $d$ and the two degree-$d$ sides are consequently the ones prescribed by $F_u^0,F_u^1$ on the respective poles. The sole additional label at degree $d+1$ is $r$, and all higher rows come from $c$. These are precisely the rows of the asserted composite. The elementary $R(z)$ itself is obtained by using the common lower rows, its two specified caps and the single top label. Their difference is $\partial r$ by the homotopy formula, since $K$ vanishes below the cut and hits only $S$ there. This also shows that for $j\le t$ the image of the native atom $A_pB_j^y$ is the $s$-face of the atom $A_mB_j^y$. Indeed that face has top first tag $s$, the indicated other site when $j>0$, and collapses to the native old atom. The tag calculation gives all its lower rows.

### A sufficient condition involving only old variables

Start with an old section compatible with ordinary signed face inclusions. In the new presentation, suppose we can type substitutions $\sigma_t^e$ for the old generators with strict images $F_t^e$. Require the ordinary inclusions at $t=0$ and identical named variables below first rank $p$. Denote the image of $S=A_pB_j^y$ at its own threshold by $Y_j^e(S)=\sigma_j^e(S)$. Require that its image for any $t>j$ be literally $Y_j^{y\oplus(p\bmod2)}(S)$, and declare the new $A_mB_j^y$ with faces $Y_j^0(S),Y_j^1(S)$. The latter terms are parallel since the two substitutions at threshold $j$ agree on the attaching data of $S$.

In a fixed or signed sector of the classification take the old term or its indicated substitution instance. In a crossing, use the depth-zero action on $T(z)$ at its maximal variable $S$. Substitute $\sigma_u^0$ on old variables, the term $Y_u^1(S)$ for the new endpoint of $S$, and the actual variable $r$ for its change. This gives a term for $R(z)$ with exactly the two required sector terms as faces. The two substitutions agree on every other variable in the support of $z$, by the cutoff and the stationary-tag calculation. The action has strict top chain $r$ because $S$ occurs once in the degree-$d$ chain of $z$. For $c$ above degree $d$, take $T(c)[\sigma_u^0]*_d R(z)$, using the usual binary whiskering heads with recursive faces. For $c=z$ use $R(z)$ directly. The new atom itself has $z=S$, so that this action is literally its declared variable.

To see why this gives a *single* assignment, first take a face in a sector. As long as the old face contains a column label at transverse rank at least $t$, the same substitution applies. Otherwise let $j<t$ be its highest column rank. The signed label $A_pB_j^y$ there is stationary since it has no column successor. On its entire support the substitution now agrees literally with $\sigma_j^{y\oplus(p\bmod2)}$. This is precisely the sector selected for that face. Faces missing the column are fixed. For a crossing with $\dim c>d+1$, both faces are crossings with the same terminal $d$-pole, so the recursive whiskering heads give the required terms. At $\dim c=d+1$ the two exterior faces are the initial sector at the source and the final sector at the target. At $\dim c=d$ these are the two faces of the action itself. Identity towers use the ordinary iterated identity terms. All ordinary signed atoms are pinned by $\sigma_0^e$. This proves the criterion without requiring comparisons between different serializations.

There is a triangular way to pose its input. At stage $t\ge1$ assume $\sigma_{t-1}^e$ available, and use $Z=A_pB_t^y$. Each $(p+t-1)$-pole $z_\alpha$ of $Z$ has a single stationary column variable of transverse rank $t-1$, with tag $\alpha\oplus(p\bmod2)$. Its change gives $R(z_\alpha)$ by the previous paragraph at threshold $t-1$. The two native cap terms can be taken to be
\[
 Y_t^0(Z)=\sigma_{t-1}^0(Z)*_{p+t-1}R(z_1),
 \qquad
 Y_t^1(Z)=R(z_0)*_{p+t-1}\sigma_{t-1}^1(Z).
\]
Their faces are exactly $T(z_0)[\sigma_{t-1}^0]$ and $T(z_1)[\sigma_{t-1}^1]$. These are also the lower substitutions required at threshold $t$, by the tag calculation. The extra top label from $R$ has exactly the transverse tag in $F_t^e$. We may therefore declare $A_mB_t^y$ immediately. What still needs a construction at stage $t$ is the image of each old $A_pB_j^y$ with $j>t$, in ascending $j$, between the two substituted attaching terms. Its prescribed top chain has just the signed old generator. This is a sufficient list of generator judgments for all globe pairs. It does not require retaining the choices of Response 4.

In particular, if $(p,2)$ is absent, only $t=0,1$ are needed, and at $t=1$ there is no old column generator above the threshold. The construction gives CT on this whole subpresentation from CT on the corresponding old subpresentation. At an independent horizontal $(d+1)$-step with stationary $S$ in degree $d$, depth-one naturality at $S$ compares taking the right change before or after that step, with the actual four terms of this assignment. The theorem applies on the support subcontext because $S$ there is maximal and all other higher-dimensional generators in the step have first rank less than $p$. For the general criterion there can still be old column generators above the threshold. The existence of their typed images, and hence the all-dimensional Q2 assignment, remains open. [Naturality for higher-dimensional path types, Theorem 3.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

The checker reconstructs all 8,987 intrinsic cells in the five-by-five box, including 1,683 crossings. Through bounds eight it verifies 83,840 signed basis images, 41,920 homotopy equations and both horizontal and both vertical boundaries on 182 distant squares. For the subpresentation of $D^3\otimes D^4$ omitting $(2,2)$, it independently evaluates all 488 raw terms as strict tableaux, checks every literal face and checks all 48 generator pins. It also emits the crossing judgments in dimensions four and five for Agda. [Threshold reconstruction and term checker](../../../tools/gray_globe_threshold_faces.py)

~~~sh
python3 tools/gray_globe_threshold_faces.py 8 --terms 4 --agda /tmp/GrayThresholdFaces.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayThresholdFaces.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 10

**The image at transverse rank $j=t+1$ in Response 9 can always be constructed from the preceding threshold.** This holds for arbitrary old attaching terms and every first rank $p$. Hence the remaining generator judgments have $j\ge t+2$. There is also a choice of crossing order that makes a section built this way equivariant for total opposition. Together the two results leave one signed judgment per growth step for $D^M\otimes D^3$. That judgment uses only the old signed generators and the new *pure* generators. Its two boundary comparisons can be posed without the old top generator.

### Moving from one threshold to the next

Use the notation of Response 9. Fix $t\ge1$, put $d=p+t-1$, and suppose the preceding substitution $E=\sigma_{t-1}^e$ has been constructed on the required native box. Write $M=\sigma_t^e$ for the substitution being constructed. In degree $d$ precisely one old variable changes between the two substitutions,
\[
 x=A_p B_{t-1}^{(1-e)\oplus(p\bmod2)}.
\]
The new generator $r=A_{p+1}B_{t-1}^{(1-e)\oplus(p\bmod2)}$ goes from $E(x)$ to $M(x)$ for $e=0$ and from $M(x)$ to $E(x)$ for $e=1$. Every variable below degree $d$ has the same image. For a native column atom of transverse rank $j\ge t$, the $d$-pole on side $1-e$ contains $x$ once and the opposite $d$-pole omits it. This follows from the two transverse tags $\alpha\oplus(p\bmod2)$ of the native $d$-row. Applying the depth-zero action to the term of the affected pole at $r$ gives the cell $C$ of degree $d+1$ used below.

There is a cut identity in every higher degree. For the native atom or any of its poles $w$ of dimension greater than $d$, let $E_s,M_s$ denote the two strict maps. Then
\[
 M_s(w)=
 \begin{cases}
 E_s(w)*_d C_s & e=0,\\
 C_s *_d E_s(w) & e=1 ,
 \end{cases}
\]
where $C_s$ is the strict value of $C$. Indeed the two maps agree below $d$. At $d$ exactly the indicated exterior pole changes. In each signed $(d+1)$-row the unique old $(p,t)$ site contributes the extra $(p+1,t-1)$ label of $C_s$. Above this row the maps agree on each basis label. This proves equality of all tableau rows, including when the two immediate poles of $w$ have different top labels. Write $D(w)$ for the binary CaTT whiskering with this same order applied to $E(T(w))$ and $C$. Its higher faces are the corresponding whiskerings of the old faces.

For $j=t$ the new image is exactly $D(w)$ on the atom, by the triangular construction in Response 9. Take $j=t+1$ and write $g=A_pB_{t+1}^y$, with attaching terms $u,v$ of degree $d+1$. In the old context through degree $d+1$ take the up-closure of $x$. The only possible proper cofaces in this closure are the variables $Z=A_p B_t^{y'}$. This follows by support and the upper bound $p$ on first rank. The up-closure and both terms therefore have depth at most one. For $e=0$ label the two endpoints of this naturality context by $E,M$; for $e=1$ label them by $M,E$. Supply $r$ at $x$. At each marked $Z$ supply an identity. This is well typed because the affected $d$-pole is the only marked boundary and $M(Z)$ was defined using exactly the binary composite on the other side of its change type.

Depth-one naturality applied to $w=u,v$ gives comparisons
\[
 c_w:D(w)\longrightarrow M(w)\quad(e=0),\qquad
 c_w:M(w)\longrightarrow D(w)\quad(e=1).
\]
Here $M(w)$ means ordinary substitution in the *given* attaching term. The action at the affected pole is the same $C$ for $u$ and $v$ by globularity. The other pole contributes no factor. The $c_w$ are invertible. In a context of the two endpoint substitutions through degree $d+1$ and $r$, all top changes for the $Z$ have been instantiated by identities, so these are $(d+2)$-terms over the $(d+1)$-skeleton. The boundary formula and dimension hypotheses are those of [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, §3 and Theorem 3.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620).

Whisker $E(g)$ by $C$ to make $D(g):D(u)\to D(v)$. With products at cut $d+1$ choose
\[
 M(g)=
 \begin{cases}
 c_u^{-1}\,D(g)\,c_v & e=0,\\
 c_u\,D(g)\,c_v^{-1} & e=1 .
 \end{cases}
\]
Its type has literal endpoints $M(u),M(v)$. The comparisons contribute no strict top chain, and the cut identity gives the required strict value. This proves the extension through $t+1$. In particular the extension of an old signed section in Response 9 works on the entire subpresentation omitting $(p,3)$: threshold one reaches transverse rank two by this argument, and threshold two then constructs its native caps. Arbitrarily high cells at smaller first rank are allowed there.

### The next gap uses only naturality on two poles

For $j=t+2$ and $e=0$, put $g=A_pB_{t+2}^y$, and let $U,V$ be its immediate attaching terms, of degree $d+2$. They have the same two immediate poles $i,j'$ of degree $d+1$. Set $A=c_i$ and $B=c_{j'}$. Use juxtaposition here for cut $d+1$. It suffices to construct two invertible terms
\[
     H_W:D(W)B\longrightarrow A\,M(W)\qquad(W=U,V).
\]
All terms in their types are already defined without $g$. These are the specialized depth-two naturality judgments for the same up-closure in the old $(d+2)$-skeleton. In particular there is no extra variable-change obligation at degree $d+2$. At a marked variable $Z$ of that degree the construction above gives $M(Z)=c_{sZ}^{-1}D(Z)c_{tZ}$. Reassociation, inverse cancellation and the unit laws thus supply the required invertible change from $D(Z)c_{tZ}$ to $c_{sZ}M(Z)$. At smaller degrees use exactly the components already described. Only the two *term* judgments on $U,V$ remain in this argument.

To verify sufficiency, whisker $D(g)$ on the right by $B$. Change its endpoints by $H_U^{-1}$ and $H_V$, now composing at cut $d+2$. This gives a cell from $A M(U)$ to $A M(V)$. Whisker on the left by $A^{-1}$. At each endpoint reassociate, cancel $A^{-1}A$ and apply the unit comparison. These are differences of writing with invertible witnesses and give a term with precisely the endpoints $M(U),M(V)$. The strict top chain comes only from $D(g)$, provided the two supplied terms are taken in the lower skeleton as required. This supplies the generator image. The same proof with the opposite order applies at $e=1$. Depth-one naturality alone does not give the displayed terms for arbitrary $U,V$.

### One sign and the transverse strip of width one

The threshold construction can use the total opposition of Response 8. At the strict level it takes $F_t^e$ to $F_t^{1-e}$ because both bit tags in the formula of Response 9 are complemented. For the crossing case of that response, the elementary change on $r$ can also be performed *first*, at the initial $d$-pole, followed by the positive sector. This gives the same strict cell: the $d$-row runs between the same two exterior sectors, the next signed rows each add $r$, and all higher rows agree. Choose the change last if the transverse tag of the stationary label is zero, and first if the tag is one. The tag stays fixed on every higher face. A star tag at this crossing forces $u=n$ and the collapsed cell contains the top $(p,n)$ generator. That cell is the native atom, so its change is just the variable. This also proves compatibility when an ordinary signed face inclusion sends the star to a bit.

Starting with an opposite-equivariant old section, the two orders are now exchanged under reflection. In constructing the substitutions choose the negative sign in the minimal native box and take its total opposite for the other sign, complementing the label tags. The triangular caps on the two sides are reflected composites, with the same reflected marked-leaf actions as in Response 8. On variables below the threshold the required sign $y\oplus(p\bmod2)$ changes to its complement; hence these paired choices obey the literal restriction rule as well. The two sectors of a reflected cell use precisely the paired substitutions. This gives equivariance inductively, including when a supplied proof is used at both ordinary signed copies of a native label.

At the first growth step, $p=1$, all the threshold substitutions exist with the signed strip declarations used in Response 5. For each $t$ start at right bound $t$, where Response 9 gives the native caps from the preceding threshold. To grow the right bound from $b\ge t$ to $b+1$, use the two ordinary signed old strips in the target together with the new first-rank-two variables of transverse rank less than $t$. These are all the variables the images use. Mark transverse rank $b$ on source and target. The marked ranks in both are $(0,b),(1,b)$ and the variables added at first rank two remain unmarked. Every marked old image contains its one signed marked top label. At $b=t$ the extra summand in the image has transverse rank $t-1$ and is unmarked. All other images are free of marks. Thus both contexts and the substitution meet the depth-one hypotheses, with exactly the desired inverse image of the marks.

Use naturality of the substitution for transverse growth, as in the strip argument of Response 5. The two signed restrictions agree literally by the endpoint substitution equations. The changed mixed image has precisely its one raised signed label in top degree: its old image had that label once, and any additional top summand was unmarked. Equivalently, the transverse column homotopy sends that mixed label with the Leibniz sign to the same raised label on either route; it kills the extra summand at $b=t$. The right-slot variance uses the opposite convention of Response 5 on both sides. Thus the raised term has the prescribed strict image and the required substituted faces. The target declarations in the marked layer are just the ordinary signed strip declarations, since the first-rank-two parameters use lower transverse ranks. This induction can construct one sign and take its opposite for the other. The width-one source section can itself be obtained by growing the transverse globe: the only native declarations to make are the pure change and the mixed change between the signed variable and its pure boundary action, precisely these strip declarations. The crossing order above, with the axes exchanged, supplies the section on composite cells.

Consequently, for $D^M\otimes D^3$ the threshold criterion has just **one residual generator image per $2\le p<M$**. Take $e=0,t=1$ and the native label $A_pB_3$ in its minimal right box. The target of this judgment uses the two signed old sections and the new pure $(p+1,0)$ variables only. Threshold zero is ordinary inclusion. At threshold one the preceding proof reaches rank two; at threshold two it reaches rank three once threshold one is available there; and threshold three only needs its native caps. For arbitrary transverse bound $n$, the same reasoning leaves at most $(n-1)(n-2)/2$ inputs per $p\ge2$ for $n\ge2$, one for each $1\le t\le j-2$ and $j\le n$. Neither the two depth-two pole comparisons for arbitrary $p$ nor the longer gaps have been proved here, so Q2 remains open.

The checker compares the whole adjacent-threshold cut product on native atoms and all their intermediate poles. It also checks the transverse growth identities and the two opposite crossing orders. A separate syntax calculation starts from formal parameters for the *preceding* substitutions, with their actual attaching types and strict tableaux. On the width-one strip it constructs the next two substitution instances, including the reassociation and the exchange past an independent whiskered step, and checks every resulting face and strict image. At threshold two Agda checks the corrected rank-three images at their substituted types. [Adjacent threshold checker and certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_adjacent_thresholds.py)

~~~sh
python3 tools/gray_globe_adjacent_thresholds.py 8 --terms 2 --agda /tmp/GrayAdjacentThresholds.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayAdjacentThresholds.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 11

**Arbitrary-depth naturality on ordinary globular pasting arities implies CT for every pair of globes.** For a fixed transverse bound $n\ge 2$, depth at most $n-1$ in arbitrary dimension suffices. In this implication every upper generator image and its change are constructed together. The only naturality inputs are on the old attaching terms. There is also an unconditional reduction of the hypothesis. At any depth, a generic naturality judgment whose selected variables are all maximal leaves has a CaTT filler as soon as its lower boundary actions are supplied.

### Lifting through the source whiskerings

Fix the negative threshold of Response 10, with $E=\sigma_{t-1}^0$, $M=\sigma_t^0$, $d=p+t-1$, and the distinguished change $r:E(x)\to M(x)$. Use the mixed-dimensional binary composites given by the unbiased operation on the wedge of two discs. Let $g=A_pB_j^y$, $k=p+j$, and write $s_i,v_i$ for its old source and target terms in dimension $i<k$. Write $H_w$ for the specialized partial naturality cell on an old term $w$. At degree $d$ the only factor is $C=H_{v_d}$, the depth-zero action at $r$. All poles $s_i,v_i$ with $i>d$ have this same pair of $d$-poles. They meet the up-closure of $x$.

For an old pole $w$ above degree $h$, or for $w=g$, define the following two operations through level $h$. In the right column $z$ is a candidate with the two $M$-substituted faces of $w$.
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 Q_d(w)=E(w)*_d C, & P_d(z)=z,\\
 Q_i(w)=Q_{i-1}(w)*_i H_{v_i},&
 P_i(z)=H_{s_i}*_i P_{i-1}(z)\qquad(d<i\le h).
\end{array}
\]
The $s_i,v_i$ in either formula are the common lower poles of the cell being padded. For $k\ge d+2$ put $h=k-2$, $s=s_{k-1}$ and $v=v_{k-1}$. The two ordinary naturality judgments on the immediate attaching terms give
\[
 H_s:Q_h(s)\to P_h(M(s)),\qquad
 H_v:Q_h(v)\to P_h(M(v)).
\]
Here $M(s)$ and $M(v)$ are actual substitutions into the specified terms. The formulas follow by reading the source and target factors in order of increasing cut, omitting the unmarked poles. All such $H_w$ for $\dim w>d$ are invertible when constructed in the target skeleton through $\dim w$. They have degree $\dim w+1$ there. In particular they have identity strict image. The labeling of the marked context needs this skeleton property only **after** specializing its change variables.

Use cut $k-1$ in the following product:
\[
             b= H_s^{-1}\;\bigl(Q_h(g)\; H_v\bigr).
\]
Its exact endpoints are $P_h(M(s)),P_h(M(v))$. To recover a $k$-cell between $M(s)$ and $M(v)$, peel the left whiskerings from $i=h$ down to $d+1$. At step $i$ the given cell goes from $P_i(M(s))$ to $P_i(M(v))$. Left whiskering by $H_{s_i}$ lifts it, up to an invertible comparison, to a cell with the **specified** faces $P_{i-1}(M(s)),P_{i-1}(M(v))$. Indeed, in the iterated hom with the fixed lower faces, $H_{s_i}$ is an invertible arrow. Essential $\omega$-surjectivity of its whiskering map applies between any prescribed parallel inputs in any dimension. It also preserves invertible comparisons. This applies inside the term algebra of the target $k$-skeleton, so the preimages and comparisons are terms in that same context. The standard whiskerings in this theorem take two inputs of possibly unequal dimensions. Their instructions take the variable on a disc and the contraction of the already chosen boundary instructions on a nondisc. Interpreting the contraction by a CaTT coherence gives exactly the unbiased binary heads used above, including their literal higher faces. [Fujii–Hoshino–Maehara, Definitions 2.3.3 and 2.3.5, Remark 3.2.2 and Theorem 3.2.14](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.13240) [Benjamin–Markakis on CaTT term models and computads](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.00398)

Take the final preimage for $M(g)$. Whisker each comparison back through the preceding outer factors and compose. This gives $b\simeq P_h(M(g))$. Whiskering this equivalence by $H_s$, followed in the appropriate direction by reassociation, inverse cancellation and the unit law, gives
\[
          H_g:Q_h(g)\; H_v\longrightarrow H_s\;P_h(M(g)).
\]
This is the required variable label for extending the naturality substitution to the next degree. Its two ends have the same strict tableau since it is a $(k+1)$-term in the $k$-skeleton. On the right each source correction is strictly an identity on the face at its cut. On the left the upper target corrections are likewise identities. Thus $M(g)$ has the strict value of $E(g)*_d C$, precisely $F_t^0(g)$ by the cut identity of Response 10. The argument uses invertible whiskering only on the source corrections above degree $d$. The cell $C$ can be noninvertible.

For $j=t$ take the native choice $M(g)=E(g)*_d C$ and the identity change. Off the marked column keep the common substitution. This starts the simultaneous induction on generator dimension. At every subsequent stage the two endpoint substitutions and all the lower variable changes label the naturality context through degree $k-1$. Apply naturality there to its terms $s,v$ and run the construction just given. Restrict to the minimal native box for each label and transport along the signed inclusions. Response 10 supplies the reflected choices at the other sign, and Response 9 reconstructs the assignment on all cells from these substitutions. The disjoint degree-zero cases are ordinary globes.

To quantify the hypothesis, use the ordinary substitution recursion for naturality on a coherence head. A pulled-back mark of a slot $q$ requires that its argument depend on a marked variable of dimension at least $d$. A term of dimension below $d$ cannot do so. Every head needed for a pole of dimension $k-1$ has dimension at most $k-1$, as do its arguments and its attaching data. Thus the largest required depth is $(k-1)-d=j-t\le n-1$. Known depth-one naturality starts this induction. A family of higher generic fillers must extend its lower choices so that the substitutions in the two displayed judgments typecheck. [Ordinary partial naturality, §§1.3–1.4](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md) [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorem 3.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

### A filler when the selected variables are leaves

Let $c=\mathsf{coh}_{P,B}[\mathrm{id}]$ have dimension $m$. Suppose every selected variable in the globular arity $P$ is maximal, meaning that it occurs in the type of no other variable. The selections can have different dimensions. In $P\uparrow Y$ each selected leaf $y$ becomes two parallel leaves $y^-,y^+$ with an arrow $\widehat y:y^-\to y^+$. All its proper faces are unselected. Adding a child at each selected leaf of the tree of $P$ gives exactly this context, up to the variable order. Denote the resulting pasting arity by $R$.

Use any well-typed lower boundary actions local to the old terms on which they are taken. Locality here means that an action uses only the promoted variables in the support of its argument and their faces. The ordinary substitution recursion has this property. Form the cylinder endpoints $a_c,b_c$ by the partial naturality formula, including the copies $c^-,c^+$ respectively. They are parallel by the cylinder typing lemma. It suffices to check the CaTT support condition on $R$.

Take a marked leaf $y$ of dimension $l<m$. The strict cell of a full generic head in a globular arity uses every leaf, including $y$. Since $y$ is maximal, it can occur in its tableau support only as a label in degree $l$. No $(l+1)$-generator of $P$ has $y$ in its differential, so the two signed $l$-rows contain $y$ with the same positive coefficient. Both $l$-poles of $c$ therefore use $y$. In the cylinder type of either such pole $u$, all whiskering factors below cut $l$ come from old terms of dimension less than $l$. By locality these factors use neither copy of $y$. The source of $H_u$ consequently contains $y^-$ with positive top coefficient and $y^+$ with coefficient zero. Its target has the reverse coefficients. In $R$ the only differential with a $y^+$ coefficient is that of $\widehat y$. Hence $H_u$ uses $\widehat y$ and its typed support contains both copies. Applying this to the target $l$-pole puts all three variables in $a_c$. The source $l$-pole puts them in $b_c$.

Each endpoint already uses every unselected variable, through its copy of $c$. A selected leaf of dimension $m$ appears only in that copy, with its indicated sign, since every other factor comes from a proper pole. If there are no selected $m$-leaves, both endpoints therefore have full support in $R$. Otherwise $R$ has dimension $m+1$, and the supports are precisely its negative and positive $m$-boundaries. Its only $(m+1)$-generators are the arrows on those selected $m$-leaves. In either case the single head $\mathsf{coh}_{R,\,a_c\to b_c}[\mathrm{id}]$ supplies the exact judgment. This proof accepts the lower faces already chosen by the caller. In particular the remaining generic inputs in the globe-pair implication can be confined to depth at least two with at least one selected **nonmaximal** variable.

The first checker builds distinct raw face terms on a mixed whiskering arity, peels the successive prefixes with those faces, and interprets the result in an actual native threshold tableau. Agda checked two successive lifts at degree five and the resulting generator change. The second checker constructs the leaf fillers on branching arities, including a lower leaf selected together with a top leaf. It tests an unbiased composite, an alternative writing, their comparison and an identity on the comparison. Agda checked the mixed-leaf composite and comparison at arity height three. The general proofs above cover arbitrary dimension. Neither calculation supplies the generic fillers for incident marks at depth two and above. The unconditional globe-pair assertion in Q2 and the full assertion in Q1 remain open here. [Threshold checker](../../../tools/gray_globe_threshold_padding.py) [Leaf-action checker](../../../tools/gray_leaf_naturality.py)

python3 tools/gray_globe_threshold_padding.py 1 1 4 --agda /tmp/GrayThresholdPadding.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayThresholdPadding.agda
python3 tools/gray_leaf_naturality.py 3 --agda /tmp/GrayLeafNaturality.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayLeafNaturality.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 12

**At depth two, the only generic heads still needed by Response 11 have a generating edge between two lowest-dimensional marks.** More precisely, let $c$ be a full generic head of dimension $d+2$ on a globular pasting arity $P$, and let $Y$ be up-closed with lowest selected dimension $d$. Suppose no $(d+1)$-variable of $P$ has both its $d$-source and its $d$-target selected. Then the partial naturality judgment for $c$ has a filler, including when the caller has already chosen its lower actions. This allows internal selected vertices, incident higher generators and arbitrary additional selections above dimension $d$. In particular it narrows the uniform width-three problem to depth-two heads containing a pair of adjacent marks.

### The two depth-one actions

Put $m=d+1$, $n=d+2$, and work in the ordinary partial cylinder $C=P\uparrow Y$. Use $E,M$ for its minus and plus inclusions and $r_x:x^-\to x^+$ for the change at a selected $d$-variable. Call a variable above $d$ outgoing if its iterated $d$-source is selected, and incoming if its iterated $d$-target is selected. The hypothesis makes these classes disjoint, since the two $d$-poles of a higher variable are already the poles of its $m$-source. All higher cofaces of an outgoing variable are outgoing, and likewise for incoming variables.

Make two further substitutions $L,V$ into $C$. At dimensions through $d$ set $L=E$ and $V=M$. In higher dimensions use the following entries, with $x$ the indicated selected pole.

| Old variable $y$ | $L(y)$ | $V(y)$ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Outgoing at $x$ | $r_x *_d M(y)$ | $M(y)$ |
| Incoming at $x$ | $E(y)$ | $E(y)*_d r_x$ |
| Neither | $E(y)$ | $E(y)$ |

For an $m$-variable these have the required endpoints by the direction of $r_x$. Above $m$, taking either immediate face commutes literally with the displayed mixed whiskering. This verifies both substitutions on the declarations, since faces of variables in $P$ are variables.

For the change $E\to L$ select only the outgoing variables above $d$. For $V\to M$ select the remaining members of $Y$ above $d$. Both selections are up-closed and start at degree $m$. In the first cylinder label each selected change by its original variable $\widehat y$ in $C$. The old target $d$-factor there was absent and the old source $d$-factor is now part of $L(y)$. Every higher factor in its type is the same original change variable. In the second cylinder the old target $d$-factor on incoming variables has become part of $V(y)$, and the source $d$-factor is absent. At a selected variable in neither class there was no $d$-factor. These identities verify the change labels even at an $n$-generator. Depth-one naturality now gives actions on $c$ and its poles for both substitutions. [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorem 3.1 and Lemma 3.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

Write $s,v$ for the immediate poles of $c$. Let $Q(z)=z*_d H_{v_d}$ and $B(z)=H_{s_d}*_d z$ using the supplied actions on the target and source $d$-poles. Omit each operation when its pole is unmarked. The $d$-poles agree for $c,s,v$. Whisker the first depth-one action by $Q$, and the second by $B$. Each becomes a square between the padded caps, with corresponding whiskered arrows on $s$ and $v$. Here and below the squares are composed in the iterated hom with those fixed $d$-boundaries. To verify the precise square type, distribute the fixed $d$-whisker across each binary cut-$m$ product on its boundary. Both writings lift to the wedge of the fixed $m$-disc with a pair of $n$-discs composed at $m$ and have the same faces there. A coherence and its reverse on that arity perform the change. If a depth-one selection misses a pole, use the identity on that pole and the unit comparison. If it misses the whole head, use the unit square on the common cap.

### The intermediate pasting arity

There is one ordinary pasting diagram $R$ containing both intermediate writings. In each string of $m$-cells at cut $d$, insert $r_x$ at each selected $d$-vertex, separating $x^-$ from $x^+$. Retain the minus block before each inserted edge and the plus block immediately after it. More explicitly, keep the minus copy of each incoming block, the plus copy of each outgoing block, and the minus copy of every other block. Use this same sign on all the cells above the block. At an isolated selected $d$-leaf add one child. This is a tree construction, including when a string starts or ends at a selected vertex. The two signs never prescribe different copies of a single block. Every retained variable has its ordinary globular variable faces in this diagram. Both $L$ and $V$ take values in $R$.

For $w=s,v$ there is a comparison $e_w:Q(L(w))\to B(V(w))$ on the corresponding face of $R$. The two terms have the literal exterior $d$-poles $E(s_d),M(v_d)$. Both use every retained old leaf on the support of $w$. On the left, each inserted $r_x$ occurs in the outgoing block, or, if that block is absent, in the change of the target $d$-pole. On the right it occurs in the incoming block or in the change of the source $d$-pole. An isolated marked leaf occurs in both pole changes. In each pole change the occurrence is forced because $r_x$ is the only differential there carrying the positive copy of $x$. Thus both terms have full typed support on this promoted old face, which is a pasting arity. If a pole misses all the marks, the same argument uses its ordinary support with no insertions.

Use cut $m$ for juxtaposition. Over the full $R$ there is a square
\[
          Q(L(c))\,e_v\ \longrightarrow\ e_s\,B(V(c)).
\]
Its endpoints are parallel by the choices just made. The same support calculation applies to the full head. Each side uses the retained old leaves and all the inserted edges. Hence a single coherence on $R$ supplies this square, also when $c$ is a higher coherence on an arity of height $m$. Paste the first depth-one square, this square, and the second depth-one square. Pasting two consecutive squares means reassociating around their shared cap, whiskering each square by the remaining side, and composing at cut $n$. This gives a square from $Q(E(c))$ to $B(M(c))$ whose sides $J_s,J_v$ are the composites of the three respective side arrows.

### Agreement with the prescribed lower actions

Fix $w=s$ or $v$, and restrict to its old support. This is its signed $m$-boundary arity when the strict full cell has height $n$, and the full old arity when the head is above its height. The cylinder on this support has a particularly simple presentation over its middle diagram $R_w$. For an incoming $m$-variable keep $y^-$ and put $a=y^-*_d r_x$. For a selected $m$-variable with both poles unmarked take $a=y^-$. In either case the additional data are a fresh parallel $b=y^+$ and $h:a\to b$. For an outgoing $m$-variable keep $y^+$ and take $a=r_x*_d y^+$. The additional data are $b=y^-$ and $h:b\to a$. All anchors $a$ live in $R_w$. These are all the additional declarations since the old support has height $m$.

Compare any two local lower actions $F,G:Q(E(w))\to B(M(w))$, in particular the prescribed $H_w$ and $J_w$. First collapse every pair $h:a\to b$ by $b\mapsto a$, $h\mapsto 1_a$. This projection $p$ has a depth-one change from $p$ to the identity. Label the change of $b$ by $h$ and the change of $h$ by the unit comparison $1_a h\to h$. The source $Q(E(w))$ uses none of these fresh variables. Naturality of $F$ and $G$ at this change compares them respectively to $F[p]\,D$ and $G[p]\,D$, where $D$ is the same action on their common target. If the target is unchanged, omit $D$. These two comparisons are invertible since they have dimension $n+1$ in a context of height at most $n$.

In the resulting smaller context collapse each remaining $h:b\to a$ by the same projection formula. Now use the change from the identity to the projection, with $h$ on $b$ and $h\to h1_a$ on $h$. The target $B(M(w))[p]$ is fixed. This time naturality compares the two terms to their fully collapsed versions preceded by the same source action. Both naturality applications have depth at most one, since the lowest fresh variables have degree $m$ and $F,G$ have degree $n=m+1$. In $R_w$ the fully collapsed terms are parallel and have full typed support: their common source is $Q(L(w))$ and their common target is $B(V(w))$. A coherence over $R_w$ compares them. Whisker it back by the two common actions and compose with the invertible comparisons just obtained. This proves $F\simeq G$ with their original exact boundaries. The argument also works when either group of fresh pairs is empty.

Finally whisker the comparison $H_v\simeq J_v$ by $Q(E(c))$, paste the constructed square, and use $J_s\simeq H_s$ on the other end. The resulting judgment is precisely
\[
                    Q(E(c))\,H_v\longrightarrow H_s\,B(M(c))
\]
with the originally supplied actions. If a pole action is absent, the corresponding unit comparison implements the omission. Everything takes place on the support of the given head, so the judgment specializes on the old attaching terms of Response 11.

In a remaining depth-two head some $m$-variable $y$ must therefore have selected $d$-cells on **both** ends. Its change has type $y^-*_d r_{t y}\to r_{s y}*_d y^+$. Neither side is a fresh variable in the lower-face contraction above. This incidence can actually occur in the substitution recursion: in the rank-one strip of Response 10 at $t=1,j=3$, the positive attaching term has a subhead with an interior and the following terminal $1$-cell selected. The strip itself has its separate construction. No proof for all such incident pairs at arbitrary cut, or for the longer gaps, has been obtained here. Q1 and the unconditional Q2 remain open.

The checker constructs the depth-two action on a terminal block with two moving top cells and a preceding fixed cell. It checks a change of both lower writings and counts the two top change generators in the result. A second example inserts an internal marked vertex between two moving blocks and uses both kinds of change. At cut two Agda checked both degree-four actions at their stated types. [Separated-mark checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_separated_naturality.py)

python3 tools/gray_globe_separated_naturality.py 2 --agda /tmp/GrayTerminalNaturality.agda
python3 tools/gray_globe_separated_naturality.py 2 --interior --agda /tmp/GrayInteriorNaturality.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayTerminalNaturality.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayInteriorNaturality.agda
