# Researcher Response 13

**CT holds for $D^M\otimes D^3$ and $D^3\otimes D^M$ for every $M$.** The missing case of Response 12 follows from a uniform depth-two naturality theorem, including when both ends of an edge are marked. The reason is that the skeleton immediately below the cells to be compared is still freely generated by a *globular set*. The square between two composites first appears in the next dimension.

### Comparing the first layer of nonglobular generators

Write $\pi$ for strict interpretation. Fix $m\ge1$, put $n=m+1$, and let $\Lambda$ be a finite context of dimension at most $m$ whose positive-dimensional declarations have variables as both faces. Form $\Gamma$ by adjoining $n$-generators $z:A_z\to B_z$ between parallel $m$-terms. Assume the common $(m-1)$-source and target of $A_z,B_z$ are variables. The $m$-terms $A_z,B_z$ themselves may be composites. Then:

1. Every strict $n$-cell has a term with any prescribed parallel $m$-faces lifting its two faces.
2. Any parallel $n$-terms with the same strict image admit an $(n+1)$-term between them in $\Gamma$.

First observe that strict interpretation on $\Lambda$ has the globular boundary lifting property in every degree. Indeed write $X$ for its globular set of generators, $L$ for the free weak monad and $T$ for the free strict monad. The square of $L\to T$ on $X\to1$ is a pullback. A strict diagram in $TX$ together with a parallel pair above its boundary specifies a lifting problem for the contraction of $L1\to T1$. The resulting instruction paired with the same labelled diagram is a lift in $LX$. This argument allows repeated labels and identity diagrams. The free computad on $X$ is precisely the displayed variable-face context, and the term correspondence gives the specified raw faces. The same observation applies after adjoining one or two formal $m$-letters parallel to any $A_z$, since their faces are variables. [Fujii–Hoshino–Maehara, §2.2 and pullback (2.3.4)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2406.13240) [Benjamin–Markakis, Theorem 6.1](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2405.00398)

Fix the two exterior $(m-1)$-terms $S,T$. Take the category of $m$-terms $U:S\to T$ with arrows the $n$-terms modulo comparison by an $(n+1)$-term. These comparisons are invertible because $\Gamma$ has height $n$. Binary composition at cut $m$ descends to this quotient by whiskering comparisons, and the generic associator and unit comparisons give its category laws. Choose a representative for each strict $m$-cell with these exterior faces. The preceding boundary lifting supplies the representatives and an old $n$-arrow from each $U$ to its representative over the strict identity. These arrows are invertible in $\Lambda$. Transport all arrows to the representatives by conjugation. Every old $n$-arrow becomes an identity there: in $\Lambda$ it lies over an identity, and an old $(n+1)$-comparison identifies the transported endomorphism with the unit.

A strict elementary step on a generator $z$ has a strict $m$-context $D(X)$ with one occurrence of a formal $m$-letter parallel to $A_z$. In $\Lambda[X]$ lift $D(X)$ to $F(X):S\to T$. Its depth-zero action $F^\uparrow(z)$ is an arrow from $F(A_z)$ to $F(B_z)$ over the required step. Thus a temporal sequence of elementary steps lifts with the prescribed endpoints, using old arrows over identities at the joins and ends. This proves assertion 1, including the empty sequence.

If $G(X)$ is another lift with the same strict *formal* context, choose $J(X):F(X)\to G(X)$ in $\Lambda[X]$. Depth-one naturality of $J$ in $X$, specialized at $z$, compares the two composites
\[
                J(A_z)\,G^\uparrow(z)
           \qquad\text{and}\qquad
                F^\uparrow(z)\,J(B_z).
\]
The two $J$-factors are old. Consequently the normalized elementary arrow is independent of the lift. Keeping $X$ formal here matters, since substituting an actual face of $z$ could identify different occurrences. [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorem 3.1 and Lemma 3.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

For an adjacent exchange take the strict $m$-context with both independent slots retained. Lift it with exterior faces $S,T$ in the globular extension $\Lambda[X,Y]$. Equivalently take an instruction on the labelled strict tree of this context, as in the pullback above. Each formal letter occurs at a distinct maximal $m$-position. Raise both positions by one dimension. The resulting arity is still a globular tree. In that tree execute the two changes in either order using the single-leaf actions with the other endpoint fixed. The two resulting terms are parallel, and both have full typed support: the two changed leaves occur in the two steps, and the remaining positions occur in the outside caps. A coherence compares the orders. Substitute the two actual generators for the promoted leaves. The four sides are lifts of their respective strict elementary contexts, so independence of the lift just proved applies to all four. This proves exchange in the quotient even when different occurrences subsequently receive the same label.

Here are the details ensuring that this calculation treats arbitrary raw arrows. Over a generic $n$-head $H$ of height $n$, sequentialize its strict full cell. Lift the resulting elementary contexts using the globular $m$-skeleton of its tree, with the actual exterior $(m-1)$-terms of $H$. Join their actions with old comparisons as in assertion 1, prescribing the two immediate faces of $H$. The resulting word and $H$ have the same literal type and full support on the tree, hence there is a comparison before any substitution. For a head of smaller height all its inputs are old. After substitution into $\Gamma$, every joining comparison in the word remains old. Each other factor is the action of a linear $m$-term on a proper $n$-argument of the original substitution. To pass from that factor to elementary actions, the linear action takes comparisons to comparisons and distributes over a temporal product up to comparison. To see these laws, follow the unique top occurrence of its variable through the term. At each head on that branch graft a globe of comparisons, or a pair of composable $n$-discs, at the active maximal leaf. The two product writings have the same faces and full support on the grafted tree. Substitution and induction on the branch give the laws for the entire frame. A nested action on a single generator is the action of the substituted linear frame by the substitution law. Thus induction on the proper $n$-arguments expresses the original arrow as the evaluation of its strict word after normalization.

In the free strict polygraph every top expression sequentializes into elementary steps. Equality of two elementary steps is equality of the label and its formal lower context, and the remaining word relations are adjacent exchanges. Therefore the normalized arrow depends only on the strict cell. For parallel arrows the two endpoint conjugations agree, so cancelling them proves assertion 2. This is the dimension-independent use of the top-generator argument: the variable-face hypothesis verifies its old and formal-slot lifting requirements at the actual dimension $m$. [Ara et al., Appendix D, Propositions D.3.6, D.3.9 and D.3.12](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.00429) [Four-dimensional quotient argument, §§2–6](../answered/gray-contexts-r8-2026-11-18.md#2-normalize-in-a-quotient-category)

### Arbitrary marks at depth two

Take a full $n$-head on a pasting context $P$ with an up-closed selection beginning at $d=n-2$. Write $m=d+1$ and $C=P\uparrow Y$. Its $m$-skeleton is globular. The endpoint copies of old variables have variable faces, including when those faces are selected. The only change variables in this skeleton are the $r_x:x^-\to x^+$ on selected $d$-variables. The $n$-variables consist of ordinary endpoint variables and the changes at selected $m$-variables. For such a variable $y:u\to v$, the longest possible changing declaration is
\[
            h_y:y^- *_d r_v\ \longrightarrow\ r_u *_d y^+.
\]
Each absent endpoint change omits its factor. Both sides have the same literal $d$-faces, the variables $u^-,v^+$ (with a common copy at an unselected position). Thus the two assertions above apply to the entire $n$-skeleton of $C$, regardless of adjacency.

Use the strict partial-cylinder construction on the strict cell of the generic head, taking the known depth-zero and depth-one actions on its proper faces. The strict collapse from the full cylinder gives the cell between the strict values of the required two cap terms. For an identity image, assertion 2 directly compares the prescribed caps. For a nonidentity image, sequentialize this strict $(n+1)$-cell and realize the layers. To spell out the extra typing point, keep an $n$-slot formal for each individual layer. Its $n$-context can be lifted with prescribed $(n-1)$-faces by the construction of assertion 1: its lower skeleton is still the same globular $m$-skeleton, and the formal $n$-letter parallel to the two faces of a change of an old $n$-variable has variable $d$-poles. Indeed these are the minus source and plus target $d$-variables of that old variable. The other slots for top generators have the same property. Acting on the $(n+1)$-generator yields the layer. Assertion 2, now in the ordinary $n$-skeleton, aligns all joins and both prescribed caps over strict identities. The composite gives the required CaTT judgment and retains the chosen strict cylinder. This uses the strict cylinder on the lower actions from the ordinary recursion. [Strict collapse and layer criterion, §2 item 3](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md)

Consequently the generic choices through depth two extend the known choices at smaller depth in every dimension. In the ordinary substitution recursion, an argument of dimension below $d$ cannot depend on a selected variable of dimension $d$ or higher. Thus pulling the selection back to a subhead cannot increase the depth above two here. Together with the simultaneous generator construction in Response 11, this supplies every threshold substitution for transverse bound three. Response 9 then assigns the terms for **all** cells of $D^M\otimes D^3$, preserving generator variables, strict images and the literal faces. The zero-dimensional factors are ordinary globes. Reversing the factors with the odd-dimensional opposite gives $D^3\otimes D^M$ as well. At transverse bound four, the first remaining threshold input has $t=1,j=4$; its attaching terms can have depth three. The $n$-skeleton argument above depends on the first nonglobular declarations occurring only in its top degree. Thus Q1 and the unbounded-width assertion in Q2 remain open.

For the syntactic check take an $n$-disc with both $d$-poles marked and put a fixed spatial arrow before it at cut zero. Each moving $m$-face changes between two composites. The checker replaces a formal-slot writing by another at *both* poles, verifies the depth-one comparison at the actual change variable, and constructs the upper term with the two replacement writings as its prescribed sides. Agda checked the degree-five filler for the degree-four head at cut two. [Adjacent-mark checker and certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_double_mark.py)

~~~sh
python3 tools/gray_globe_double_mark.py 2 --agda /tmp/GrayDoubleMark.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayDoubleMark.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 14

**A lowest-dimensional mark can be arbitrarily far below the head when it is a leaf.** Let $c$ be a generic head of dimension $k\ge 3$ on a pasting arity $P$, with an up-closed selection $Y$. If every selected variable of dimension below $k-2$ is maximal in $P$, the partial naturality judgment for $c$ has a filler with the strict cylinder value and the prescribed lower actions from the ordinary recursion. Marks in the last three dimensions may have arbitrary incidences. In particular this includes an edge with both poles marked in one branch and a much lower marked leaf in another.

For the remaining width-four problem there is also a reduction to first identities in cylinders truncated two degrees above their lowest mark. Their strict cells split into ordinary two-dimensional cylinder cells. The proof below gives exact comparisons for different orders of assembling the corresponding local weak terms.

### Moving the isolated leaves first

Put $L=\{x\in Y:\dim x<k-2\}$. Every proper face of an element of $L$ is unselected, since a selected such face would be a nonmaximal member of $L$. No old variable has a member of $L$ in its type. Insert a child at each of these leaves to obtain the genuine pasting arity $R=P\uparrow L$. It contains $x^-,x^+,r_x:x^-\to x^+$ at each insertion. The remaining marks $Y'=Y\setminus L$ are still up-closed in $R$. Up to declaration order there is an equality of typed contexts
\[
                   P\uparrow Y=R\uparrow Y'.
\]
On an old variable outside $L$, its two copies and all the whiskerings in its change declaration use exactly the same signed faces on either side. None of those faces lies in $L$. At an insertion all three declarations remain fixed on the right. This accounts for every declaration, including the types of the higher changes.

Use this equality only to establish boundary lifting on the context. The caps of $c$ are still the original caps in $P\uparrow Y$, including every prescribed change at a low leaf. The $(k-1)$-skeleton of $R\uparrow Y'$ is globular. Every $k$-generator has variable $(k-2)$-poles, by the calculation in Response 13 with $d=k-2$. Thus both conclusions of its first-layer lemma hold through degree $k$. For an elementary step of a strict $(k+1)$-cell, the fresh $k$-slot parallel to its generator faces also has variable $(k-2)$-poles. For an endpoint generator they are old signed variables. For a change generator they are the minus source and plus target at that rank, since padding at every higher cut preserves them. Apply the same lemma with this slot adjoined, take its action on the actual generator, and use the ordinary $k$-skeleton comparisons to match the joins and the two prescribed caps. The empty sequence uses just that comparison. Applying this to the strict partial cylinder on $\pi c$ proves the assertion. The argument applies even when $Y'$ is empty. It requires no equation identifying a composite on $P$ with a composite on $R$.

### The next layer only needs first identities

Now let $d$ be the lowest mark, put $n=d+2$, and write $C=P\uparrow Y$, $B=C_{\le n}$ and $E=C_{\le n+1}$. Write $\mathrm{RC}(j)$ for the existence of a $(j+1)$-comparison between parallel $j$-terms of equal strict image. For these cylinders,
\[
             \mathrm{RC}(n+1)(E)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
             \mathrm{RC}(n+1)(B).
\]
The right-hand side only concerns terms whose strict images are identities on strict $n$-cells. It suffices to consider cells whose support contains a change $h_y$ of a $(d+1)$-variable $y$ with at least one marked $d$-pole.

Here are the lifting details. In $B$, Response 13 gives prescribed-boundary lifting at degree $n$ and comparison of parallel $n$-terms. Both results still hold after adjoining one or two formal $n$-letters parallel to the faces of generating steps in $E$. Their $d$-poles are variables by the signed-pole calculation above. The same holds if $E$ itself is augmented by a formal $(n+1)$-letter parallel to the faces of a generator in $C_{\le n+2}$, since iterating its faces to degree $d$ again gives the two variables. Consequently, a strict elementary $(n+1)$-step has a linear $n$-term for its formal context with prescribed $(n-1)$-poles. Its action realizes the step. Comparisons from $B$ at degree $n$ join any sequence of these steps. This proves prescribed-boundary lifting at degree $n+1$, also with that last formal letter, without assuming either side of the equivalence.

For the forward implication, a comparison in $E$ between old $(n+1)$-terms has identity strict image in degree $n+2$. Both strict faces are old. Total support therefore puts the comparison itself in $B$. For the converse take $(n+1)$-arrows modulo $(n+2)$-comparisons with the exterior $(n-1)$-poles fixed, as in Response 13. Comparisons in $B$ at degree $n$ identify the ends of elementary steps with the chosen representatives of their strict values. Under $\mathrm{RC}(n+1)(B)$ every old arrow becomes a unit after this identification. Two lifts of the same formal linear context have a comparison at degree $n$ with the slot still formal. Its depth-one naturality identifies the corresponding elementary steps in the quotient. [Total-support lemma, item 16](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md)

There is one change in the exchange argument. A two-slot $n$-term in $B[X,Y]$ need not be an instruction on one labelled globular tree of $B$. Instead follow the two formal occurrences through its raw syntax. Both occur once among the top arguments, by their separate strict multiplicities. Lower arguments cannot contain either letter. At a head where the occurrences belong to different arguments, promote those two maximal positions of the *generic* arity. In the promoted tree the two orders of their changes are parallel and use every leaf, and hence have a comparison. Substitute the two actual argument changes, keeping the other arguments fixed. Single-leaf naturality commutes with substitution, giving exactly the four actions in this exchange. At a head where both occurrences belong to the same argument, apply the result for that proper argument and the unary action at its position. This action preserves comparisons and distributes over a temporal composite up to comparison, by grafting a comparison globe or a composable pair at the one leaf as in Response 13. This proves exchange in every two-slot context. Sequentializing a generic head before substitution and then inducting on its proper top arguments, again as in Response 13, expresses every arrow as its elementary word with old arrows at the joins. Replacement and adjacent exchange present equality of strict words, which proves the converse. If the strict $n$-cell uses no indicated $h_y$, its support in $B$ consists entirely of variable-face declarations. Any parallel first-identity terms over it therefore lie in a globular-set context and have a comparison there. [Strict word presentation, Appendix D](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.00429)

It follows that $\mathrm{RC}(n+1)(B)$ suffices for the depth-three generic filler. For a nonidentity strict $(n+2)$-cylinder use the prescribed-boundary lifting just proved with an $(n+1)$-slot, take the action on each generating step, and align all joins and caps using the equivalence. For an identity cylinder the comparison suffices immediately. Response 11 then gives the width-four section if these first-identity comparisons can be constructed at every cut.

### What the branch comparisons give

For $d>0$ write $P=\bigvee_i\Sigma P_i$. Its root objects are unmarked. Between successive root objects the presentation of $B$ is the suspension of $(P_i\uparrow Y_i)_{\le n-1}$, where $Y_i$ is the restricted selection. Indeed each copy and change is confined to that branch, and every occupied cut in a change declaration increases by one under suspension. Iterating this description spends the first $d$ levels of the tree. An empty interval at a level has terminal strict hom. On a nonempty root interval $[a,b]$ the strict hom is the product of the strict categories on branches $a+1,\ldots,b$. To see this, form the strict category enriched in strict $\omega$-categories on the root path with these products as homs, identities given by empty products, and concatenation as composition. A map out is determined freely by its maps on the adjacent homs and root objects, exactly the universal property of the wedge of suspensions. Apply the same argument in each nonempty branch interval. Thus a strict $n$-cell with its outer intervals fixed is specified by a tuple of strict 2-cells in ordinary cylinder 2-skeleta. This includes identities and branches stopping before level $d$. The directed cut on raw syntax reads these coordinates one at a time. [Directed cut and its substitution law, Response 4](relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r7.md#researcher-response-4)

In each terminal 2-skeleton any two parallel 3-terms over the same 2-cell identity have a 4-comparison. These local comparisons reassemble whenever the two writings are given by the same outer tree of slots in the following sense. Take one local 3-term per active position, suspend it along its branch, and assemble the suspended terms by unbiased compositions at the outer cuts. Inactive positions carry their fixed lower-dimensional terms. Replacing each local term by a parallel one with the same strict image gives a comparison of the two assemblies. Suspend the local 4-comparisons and assemble them with the same outer cuts. At every cut the higher binary composite has as its literal faces the binary composites of the faces of its active arguments, with every lower-dimensional fixed argument retained. This proves the claim inductively from the leaves to the root. [Cylinder 2-skeleton comparison, Researcher Response §§1–7](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md#researcher-response)

One may also execute the active positions in different orders. First keep their inputs as separate formal globes, with the fixed lower positions in the same tree. Each execution step moves one input, whiskered by the sources or targets at the other positions. Use the same outer composite for each intervening stage, so its two occurrences agree literally. The resulting word and the simultaneous composite have the same literal faces and full support on the formal tree, which gives a comparison there. Substitute the local inputs. Do this on either side of the assembled 4-comparisons to connect any two such schedules, including different choices of the input writings. A single active position needs no temporal composition.

There is an exact test with one base term per choice of literal poles. Fix a strict $n$-cell $w$ and parallel $(n-1)$-terms $S,T$ lifting its poles. Take the $n$-terms $U:S\to T$ over $w$ as objects, and the $(n+1)$-terms between them modulo $(n+2)$-comparison as arrows in $B$. This is a groupoid since the arrows have dimension above the height of $B$. It is nonempty and connected by the two assertions of Response 13. Choose one object $U_0$. Comparing all parallel arrows is now equivalent to contracting every loop at $U_0$. Indeed choose a path from $U_0$ to each object and conjugate the difference of any two parallel arrows back to $U_0$. At a terminal branch all projected loops contract by the local 2-skeleton theorem. Thus the remaining question is faithfulness on these loop groups, with their literal poles fixed.

Even at the raw level the cut tuple can forget a choice. In a formal horizontal composite with two active branches, insert an initial column supported at their first object. Give this column the $(n+1)$-identity $i$ on an iterated unit or the composite $i*_n i$. The resulting parallel terms are distinct and have literally equal projections at both active branches. In this case the loop contracts: a coherence at the point compares the two labels, and the outer composite with that comparison has the required endpoints. More generally the common-frame proof allows such point-supported columns with arbitrary parallel writings, since their own comparison lives on a point. For an arbitrary head whose substitution mixes several branches, the argument still needs a comparison to a reassembled writing with its actual faces. This leaves width four, Q1 and the unbounded Q2 open.

The checker forms two suspended lax squares joined at their outer pole. In each square it changes the writing of a 3-loop that uses the two separate edges of the source path as well as the square generator. It exchanges the order of the two branches while changing both local writings. It checks the literal comparison type and applies each directed cut to the result. At $d=1$ Agda checked both the resulting 5-term and the separate comparison of the two writings with equal cut projections. At $d=1,2,3,4$ the raw checker also verified that promoting a separate low leaf first gives the identical typed cylinder. [Checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_branch_comparisons.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_globe_branch_comparisons.py 1 --agda /tmp/GrayBranchComparisons.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayBranchComparisons.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 15

**At width four, any two paths of elementary exchanges between the same serializations have a CaTT comparison, in every ambient dimension.** The serializations may use different branches at a lower cut. Each state and elementary step admits its own choice of weak term in the parameter context described below. In particular, a state term can use the two individual factors of the face of a change generator even when the adjacent step must treat that entire face as one input. The strict part classifies the top-cut factorizations of every cell of every finite Gray tensor by order ideals. The weak part compares exchanging two composite blocks with exchanging their individual entries.

Use the skeleton $B$ of Response 14, write $m=d+1$, $n=m+1$, and let $L=B_{\le m}$. Fix literal parallel $(m-1)$-poles $S,T$ and a strict $n$-cell $w$ at their strict values. A serialization of $w$ means a temporal word of elementary applications of its $n$-generators, with the intervening strict $m$-states. Each $n$-generator $z:A_z\to B_z$ has variable $(m-1)$-poles, and $L$ is a globular-set context. A simultaneous application has a strict $m$-state $D(X_1,\ldots,X_r)$ with one fresh $m$-letter per distinguished occurrence, parallel to the two faces of that occurrence. Substituting its sources and targets gives the corners. Keep different occurrences distinct.

### The strict event order in every Gray tensor

There is a chain description of the underlying schedules that applies to Q1 in every dimension, including tensors with branching factors. Let $K$ have a finite strongly loop-free unital Steiner basis, $n=m+1\ge1$, and let $w$ be an $n$-cell. Write $U$ for its top chain and $s,t$ for its negative and positive $m$-rows. For a basis event $z$ in $U$ put $a_z=\partial^-z$ and $b_z=\partial^+z$. All rows are sums of distinct basis elements. Order the events by the transitive closure of
\[
                  z<z'\quad\text{if}\quad
                  \operatorname{supp}(b_z)\cap\operatorname{supp}(a_{z'})\ne\varnothing .
\]
This is a partial order by strong loop-freeness. The factorizations of $w$ at cut $m$, units included, are in bijection with its order ideals. After an ideal $I$, every subset of the minimal remaining events has a unique simultaneous formal $m$-context with the given outer rows and named markers.

To prove this, first execute all events in a linear extension of the strong basis order. Whenever $e$ occurs in $a_z$, all its producers precede $z$, since a producer precedes $e$ and $e$ precedes each consumer. The initial coefficient plus its total production, less the consumption already made, therefore suffices for this step by $t=s+\partial U\ge0$. Each intermediate row $s+\partial I$ is nonnegative and has the same differential as $s$, writing $I$ also for its sum. Keeping the lower rows of $w$ gives an actual $m$-tableau. For $m=0$ the augmentation is unchanged. Its coefficients are at most one. In particular, at the cut after all producers of $e$ and before all its consumers, the initial coefficient plus the total production is at most one. Each $e$ has at most one producer and one consumer in $U$. [Steiner, Definitions 2.6–2.8 and Theorem 6.2(iii)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0403237)

For any ideal $I$, precisely the same availability calculation gives a cut with intermediate row $s+\partial I$. The two factors have the old rows below $m$, respectively the $m$-rows $(s,s+\partial I)$ and $(s+\partial I,t)$, and respective top chains $I$ and $U-I$. Conversely any factorization partitions $U$ by nonnegativity. If it included a consumer without its producer, the intermediate coefficient would be negative because the initial coefficient at a produced label is zero. The partition is thus an ideal cut and the factor tableaux are forced. Distinct ideals give distinct states: for a minimal event in $I\setminus J$, all its predecessors belong to $J$. Any one of its input labels is still present after $J$ and has been consumed after $I$. Input rows are nonempty by unitality. This also covers an empty top chain.

At a state $q=s+\partial I$, the input rows of a set $A$ of enabled events are disjoint. For each such $z$ adjoin an $m$-letter $X_z$ parallel to its two strict faces. For $m>0$ give it differential $\partial a_z=\partial b_z$; for $m=0$ give it augmentation one. In the truncated complex the new basis is still strongly loop-free and unital. Its iterated signed rows below $m$ are precisely the corresponding rows of $z$. For strong order place $X_z$ at the position of $z$ in a linear extension of the original basis order. Every negative term of its differential precedes that position by a chain of negative faces of $z$; every positive term follows it by a chain of positive faces. Multiple fresh copies may be placed there consecutively. The middle-interval formal globe also preserves these properties: use two such letters at that position with the new globe between them in the order. The augmented differential and the signed atom rows verify unitality of the globe as well.

Now replace the top $m$-row $q$ by
\[
                  q-\sum_{z\in A} a_z+\sum_{z\in A} X_z .
\]
It is nonnegative with the same differential or augmentation. Together with the unchanged lower rows it is an $m$-tableau in the marked complex, hence a strict composition context by freeness on Steiner atoms. It uses each named marker once. Evaluation at its source or target is the indicated row substitution, so it gives precisely the requested corners. Conversely restoring the sources forces this marked row and every lower row, proving uniqueness. In particular *all* paths of top-cut factorizations in any iterated Gray tensor are linear extensions with these simultaneous contexts. [Steiner, Theorems 5.7 and 6.1 and Example 3.10](https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0403237)

### Three intervals for each event

Return to the cylinder skeleton $B$ with $m=d+1$. Subdivide an event coordinate as $0\to1\to2\to3$. The strict generator acts on the middle interval. The two outside intervals adjust the writing at its source and target. The following table specifies a *globular* parameter context for every tile. Start with $L$ and use a separate set of fresh letters in each coordinate. All fresh $m$-letters have the variable poles of $A_z$.

| Coordinate of the tile | Data for that coordinate | Interpretation of its endpoint positions |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vertex 0 or 3 | No new letter | $A_z$ or $B_z$, respectively |
| Vertex 1 or 2 | A fresh $m$-letter | The letter, representing the indicated face |
| Interval $0\to1$ | No new letter | Both positions give $A_z$ |
| Interval $1\to2$ | $x^-,x^+$ and $h:x^-\to x^+$ | $x^-,x^+$ |
| Interval $2\to3$ | No new letter | Both positions give $B_z$ |

There is a term substitution from face data to tile data. For example the letter at vertex 1 maps to the actual composite $A_z$ in a preparation interval and to $x^-$ in a middle interval. Substitutions for different coordinates commute literally, since they fix $L$ and use disjoint fresh letters. Evaluation in $B$ sends $x^-,x^+,h$ to $A_z,B_z,z$. The two ways of evaluating any face agree as raw substitutions. At an outside vertex the old coordinate disappears from the marked state, by substitution of its actual face in $D$. This identifies the tile data on shared faces of different simultaneous applications. One can index a shared face by its strict state with the remaining occurrences still formal.

Choose an $m$-term with poles $S,T$ in each vertex context over its strict marked state. Make a single choice for each shared vertex. On each little edge take its two previously chosen terms by the face substitutions. In the edge context the strict label between them is the identity in an outside interval, or the action of the formal $h$ in a middle interval. These labels have exactly the two required faces. The globular-set boundary lifting of Response 13 therefore gives an $n$-term with the specified literal endpoints. Its evaluation gives the actual step. Composing the three steps at cut $m$ gives a lift of each original elementary event. At an original state the term may be prescribed arbitrarily with the stated strict value and poles. At a middle step the edge term may likewise be prescribed in its formal context with its stated faces. This is where the two extra intervals matter.

For a little square take its four previously chosen edges into the square context. The two products are parallel and have the same strict value. This follows from interchange in $D$ when both middle letters move and from the identity laws otherwise. Boundary lifting gives an $(n+1)$-comparison there. Its strict value is an identity, since the parameter context has height at most $n$. Use this cell for the oriented swap, and its weak inverse for the reverse swap.

For a little three-dimensional tile, take the six chosen face comparisons into its context. The two three-swap routes from order $123$ to $321$ have the same literal source and target after fixing the temporal bracketing. To place a swap inside a word, reassociate, whisker by the unchanged factors, and restore the fixed bracketing. The associators can always be taken on the formal temporal string. Both routes in the tile have identity strict value on the same strict product. Boundary lifting in this globular context supplies their $(n+2)$-comparison. Evaluating it uses precisely the comparisons already chosen on the six shared faces. The argument also works when a preparation or final adjustment has distinct raw endpoints of equal strict value.

### From little squares to arbitrary schedules

For an exchange of two original events, commute their three-step blocks by the nine little squares. For three simultaneous original events, the two orders of exchanging the blocks are strings of swaps of the nine little steps. Orient these swaps by decreasing block number, keeping the internal order of each block. Each swap reduces the inversion number. Branchings of two disjoint swaps commute by interchange on a temporal string with the two comparisons as formal inputs. For overlapping swaps the three increasing block numbers are distinct, and the three next steps occupy one little tile of the $3\times3\times3$ subdivision. Its just-constructed comparison is the braid. Induction on the inversion number compares all routes to the sorted word: for distinct first swaps use their square or braid to reach a common shorter remainder and apply induction. Cancellation against the reversed route uses the inverse and unit comparisons. Thus the original three-event braid holds with the nine-square exchanges, also after whiskering by a prefix or suffix.

For arbitrary $w$, fix a linear extension of its event order and sort towards it by adjacent swaps. Two inverted consecutive events are incomparable. An overlapping branching consists of three consecutive events in reverse order relative to the extension. They are pairwise incomparable, and all are enabled before the first of them. The marked tableau constructed above gives their simultaneous application. Two disjoint swaps again commute on a temporal string. The same inversion induction compares every exchange path to the route through the sorted word, including paths having no common simultaneous application of all their events. This proves the stated comparison in $B$, whose partial cylinder presentation is strong Steiner. More generally the weak proof works for any finite strong Steiner presentation whose $m$-skeleton is globular and whose $(m+1)$-generators have variable $(m-1)$-poles. These hypotheses make every tile parameter context globular. [Partial-cylinder basis and atom calculation](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-2026-11-19.md#verification-of-the-researcher-response-2026-11-19)

The same conclusion allows changes of the tile choices. For each tile take its product with a two-vertex interval along which the strict marked state is constant. Use the same parameter context all along this extra interval and the identity substitution in the new direction. Prescribe the two given systems at its ends. The vertex, edge, and square lifting arguments construct the connecting arrows, squares, and three-tile comparisons, respectively. At the fixed ends of a path with identical choices take identities and the unit comparisons. Pasting this strip compares the evaluated paths with the endpoint changes included. In particular changing the drawing of an elementary exchange while preserving its common edge and vertex data gives the same class.

### Refining a whole input block

There is a substitution test for the resulting comparisons. Work in a globular reference context for two independent formal $n$-letters. Replace the letters by temporal words of lengths $p,q\ge1$, with their respective $m$-endpoints fixed. The words may be histories spanning several actual addresses. In the reference context first replace each letter by the temporal composite of an abstract chain of the indicated length. Each chain has fresh $m$-letters for its interior states, all with the original variable $(m-1)$-poles, and an $n$-globe for each link. The expanded reference context is still globular. Its substitution to the target sends each link to the given step and each knot to the common literal face of successive steps.

In the $p\times q$ grid, use only the current formal knot of a stationary chain and the current globe of a moving chain, together with the fixed lower parameters. These smaller contexts include into the expanded one and agree on their common faces. Choose lifts for the grid vertices, edges and elementary squares there as above. At the four exterior corners use the whole-block corner terms with their letters replaced by the indicated endpoint knots. Along each outside edge the original evaluated block edge and the corresponding fine path have the same two $m$-faces and strict $n$-value. Join them by a track in the expanded edge context. After inserting these four side comparisons, the whole-block exchange and the $pq$-swap path are parallel tracks in the expanded reference context. Both have identity strict value, so an $(n+2)$-comparison there proves refinement after substitution. If a link is a preparation or a final adjustment, its actual strict value can be an identity. If the link carries a marked event, its own tile supplies the actual knot and globe images. The construction uses no assumption that the two words occupy consecutive intervals in a *global* address order.

### A uniform sufficient typing test for Q1

Suppose the terms through rank $m\ge1$ have been chosen. For a single enabled $z$ with marker $X$, take a term $F(X)$ for the marked row above with the prescribed exterior $(m-1)$-faces. It suffices to compare $F(T(a))$ to $T(q)$ and $F(T(b))$ to $T(q')$ over strict identities in the old $m$-skeleton. Here $a,b$ denote the *whole* two $m$-faces of the atom, $q,q'$ the whole states before and after its execution, and $F(T(a))$ means substitution for $X$. The marker is declared parallel to $T(a),T(b)$. Both tests are literally parallel because inserting it leaves the exterior rows fixed. A section at rank $m$ on the marked skeleton with the same lower terms supplies $F$. This auxiliary strict skeleton is itself strong Steiner by the preceding construction.

Indeed the depth-zero action of $F$ at the actual generator gives $F(T(a))\to F(T(b))$. The two old comparisons are invertible by dimension. Pre- and postcompose in the required directions, and concatenate along any fixed linear extension of the event order. Each resulting term has the designated literal faces and the tableau of $w$. Take the declared variable on an atom and the weak identity on an identity. This proves the rank-raising step using only the stated single-marker tests. Applying it at all ranks on flattened tensor labels would give the same assignment for all parenthesizations. The strict ideal theorem supplies the marker contexts and identifies their two evaluation maps in every dimension. The existence of the two weak comparisons for a compatible choice of sections in arbitrary rank is still required.

For the test below, three independent changes are genuine generators on suspended lax squares. At the outside vertices the $m$-terms insert a unit inside a two-factor generator boundary. At the neighboring inside vertices they use that boundary as a single input. The checker verifies the 144 little edges, including their strict top-generator counts, and both three-swap paths on each of the 27 tiles. This passed at cuts one and two. Agda checked the degree-five comparison on the tile having a preparation, a generating step and a final adjustment. It also checked a block exchange with two different physical addresses in one reference block, including both boundary distributors. [Checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_subdivided_exchanges.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_globe_subdivided_exchanges.py 1 --agda /tmp/GraySubdividedExchanges.agda
agda --safe -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GraySubdividedExchanges.agda
~~~

An independent strict check enumerated cells by composition of atoms in both the original and marked presentations. On $D^5\otimes D^5$ it checked all 8,983 positive-dimensional cells, 31,768 ideal cuts and 35,857 nonempty simultaneous marked steps, including triples. On the four-cube it checked all 505 positive-dimensional cells, 1,676 cuts and 1,271 marked steps. [Ideal and marker checker](../../../tools/gray_steiner_event_ideals.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_steiner_event_ideals.py 5 5
python3 -B tools/gray_steiner_event_ideals.py 1 1 1 1
~~~

To get the width-four section from Response 14 one still needs to identify an arbitrary raw first-identity term with a path of the comparisons above after normalizing its endpoints. The block refinement proves the exchange comparison when inputs are already temporal words. Substitution compatibility of a normalization for arbitrary raw inputs remains open, as do Q1 and the unrestricted Q2.

# Researcher Response 16

**For globe pairs in every dimension, there is a word choice for which every mixed attachment reduces to a singleton whose positive face has a unique event order.** On the negative face there are two decreasing strings of consecutive first ranks, with exactly one precedence edge between them. All other events factor off with the same *labelled* elementary terms on the two faces. Up to signed face inclusions the numbers of these mixed inputs in degrees two through seven are 1, 4, 14, 52, 211 and 920. The reduction keeps arbitrary lower-dimensional padding. It gives a smaller set of CaTT judgments for Q2.

### Isolating the two strings

Fix degree $d\ge1$. Give each $d$-event its first rank as index. On each strict $d$-cell take the lexicographically first legal word, with fixed temporal bracketing and the chosen elementary terms at their actual states. The degree-$d$ sites are distinct. Precedences are edges between consecutive occupied indices, one direction per adjacent pair. Indeed only neighbors can change labels at the same site in degree $d-1$, and at that site the two tag changes form a directed path of length two. Gaps impose no relation. The lexicographic word is obtained by partitioning the occupied indices into maximal intervals on which all edges point left, listing the intervals from left to right, and reading each interval from right to left. To execute the least outstanding index one must first execute the part of its left-pointing interval to its right. The largest index in that interval is enabled, and no smaller one is. The same reasoning gives the rest of that interval before the next one. [Signed row and notch formula, Response 17](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-17)

Consider a $(d+1)$-singleton $w$ with mixed generator at $(p+1,d-p)$. Its two faces have the same occupied indices. Between $p$ and $p+1$ the edge points right on the negative face and left on the positive face. Every other edge retains its orientation. In the Leibniz differential only the two labels at that join change. Their tags at their exterior neighbors stay fixed, while their two increments at the shared site occur in the opposite order. On the positive face let $[l,r]$ be the maximal left-pointing interval containing the join. With $[v:u]$ denoting $v,v-1,\ldots,u$, the two words on this interval are
\[
       W^-=[p:l]\,[r:p+1],\qquad W^+=[r:l].
\]
Both have the very same prefix and suffix outside the interval. In particular $W^+$ is forced on the interval even without the tie-breaking rule.

Group $[l,r]$ as one block at cut $d-1$ in $w$, assigning the unique $(d+1)$-label to that block. Keep each outside $d$-event as a separate factor. The two ends of the interval have the same exterior precedence edges on both faces. After contracting the interval their first legal order is precisely the displayed prefix, the new block, and the displayed suffix. At each block boundary the cut row is the initial row plus the differentials of its executed $d$-events. This holds on both faces, since the two $d$-chains inside the block differ by the differential of the $(d+1)$-generator and hence have the same differential themselves. The lower rows stay fixed. Therefore every exterior singleton factor agrees as an entire tableau, including its lower padding. The distinguished factor is a $(d+1)$-singleton whose sole occupied $d$-interval is $[l,r]$. Its negative and positive words expanded in place give exactly the original two sequences of *labelled singleton tableaux*. [Cut factorization and marker theorem](#researcher-response-15)

Consequently a term for the distinguished factor with the indicated word caps gives a term for $w$. Compose it at cut $d-1$ with the actual chosen exterior $d$-terms. Compare the resulting bracketings with the two prescribed bracketings on their respective formal temporal strings and specialize to those terms. Pasting the comparisons at cut $d$ gives the literal caps. The sole strict top occurrence comes from the distinguished factor. For a pure singleton only one $d$-label changes at the same index, and the order is the same on both faces. The same cut isolates its short pure input. These inputs can use the common pure action when the choices are made on minimal signed boxes. On an atom choose its variable at the word caps. Ordinary top-cut composition gives all other $(d+1)$-cells. Thus terms on the indicated mixed factors, for the choices produced at the preceding degree, suffice in every degree. [Pure action and short lifts, Response 21](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#pure-moves-in-arbitrary-dimension)

There is an exact count of the mixed factors in this test. Fix $0\le l\le p<r\le d$. A negative face has the single run $[l,r]$ with all its internal notch bits specified by the orientations above. Its two lower staircases have $s_l s_{d-r}$ choices including their tags, with $s_i$ the little Schröder numbers and $s_0=1$. Each such face determines one singleton. Insert the join above $p,p+1$, retaining the outer tag of each of its two incident labels. These determine both tags of the new generator. Its negative boundary has exactly the two given labels, because their shared-site order is the negative order. Replacing them by its positive boundary yields a positive row with the same differential and leaves every lower row fixed. Conversely the negative face recovers all the choices. This bijection respects minimal signed boxes since adding the join increases neither maximal coordinate. The count in degree $d+1$ is therefore
\[
                    \sum_{0\le l<r\le d}(r-l)\,s_l s_{d-r}.
\]
It includes atoms and instances already supplied by earlier results. [Single-run staircase enumeration, Response 22](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#two-word-choices-in-every-dimension)

### Refining a marker in the same word choice

The marker test of Response 15 can also reach *iterated* faces of atoms. If the event being marked was itself a new top letter, its immediate poles are the next iterated poles of the original atom. When passing to the lower skeleton the previous top letter disappears. For these repeated single-marker tests both specializations always insert a strictly monotone interval of events. In the degree-$d$ row of an atom of bidegree $(i,j)$ the occupied first ranks are $[\max(0,d-j),\min(i,d)]$. On its sign-$e$ face the first tag is $e$ below the first globe bound, and the second is $e\mathbin{\rm XOR}(u\bmod2)$ at first rank $u$ below the second globe bound. At the shared lower site for events $u,u+1$ the second-coordinate change precedes the first-coordinate change exactly when $e=0$. Thus the negative interval runs to the right and the positive interval to the left, including after any signed face inclusion.

In a marked top row, order the single marker at the position of the interval it occupies. Expanding it in its forced order preserves the exterior order rule on the contracted path. To check the contraction, only the two end sites can have an exterior neighbor. An exterior differential meets the input or output of the marker exactly when it met the boundary of that interval. Internal wires cancel and cannot be shared with a third event by the producer and consumer uniqueness in Response 15. This identifies the precedence graph even if one end of the interval is isolated or pure.

A left-pointing interval expands *literally in place* in the native index word, since it lies in one decreasing run. For a right-pointing interval $a_1<\cdots<a_h$, $h\ge2$, write $M=a_2\cdots a_{h-1}$. Let $L$ be the decreasing tail immediately to the left of $a_1$ attached by a left-pointing edge and $R$ the decreasing string to the right leading into $a_h$, also along left-pointing edges. Either may be empty. With their common exterior suppressed, the native word and the expanded quotient word are respectively
\[
                 a_1 L M R a_h,\qquad R a_1 M a_h L .
\]
This follows by reading the maximal decreasing intervals before and after contraction. To pass from the first word to the second, commute $R$ before $a_1LM$, then commute $L$ after $Ma_h$. Every pair across either swap is incomparable. A path of dependencies between the two sides would have to traverse the forward interval and then travel backwards through one of its ends. For $h=2$ both swaps exchange decreasing strings, and the bound of two adjacent block transpositions is sharp. With both tails nonempty, the first and last letters change, so a single such transposition would have to rotate the whole displayed substring. In the first cyclic word $a_1$ is followed by the first letter of $L$, while in the second it is followed by $a_h$. Both tails occur already on labelled rows of $D^3\otimes D^4$.

For a native mixed singleton the interval has length two. Its short lift executes the contracted block at the same state on both sides. The left-pointing face needs only rebracketing. On the other face, invertible comparisons realizing the two displayed block exchanges with the *chosen elementary terms at each state* suffice to turn the short lift into the required one. If performed by elementary swaps, this uses exactly $|R|(1+|L|)+|L|$ swaps. Each reversed pair must cross at least once, and these two operations cross each such pair once.

In particular all attachments at $(1,d)$ and $(d,1)$ in this induction have lifts, even when their two-string factor is arbitrarily long. Use minimal signed boxes and the pure actions. For $p=0$, $L$ is empty. The only exchanges move the pure step $a_1$ across the individual steps of $R$, all at indices at least two. For $p=d-1$, $R$ is empty, and the pure step $a_h$ crosses the individual steps of $L$. Each comparison at its actual state is the depth-one pure exchange, and the short lift at the join is the edge lift of round 6. Whisker those comparisons by the unchanged terms and rebracket on temporal strings. Thus only the interior joins $1\le p\le d-2$ require further mixed-factor terms. Constructing them with the specified raw endpoints remains necessary for this proof of unrestricted Q2. Q1 also remains open. [Pure exchanges and edge lifts, Response 21](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#pure-moves-in-arbitrary-dimension)

**Verification.** On $D^6\otimes D^6$ the checker reconstructed both labelled cap factorizations of all 22,649 singletons of degree at least two. It checked 50,548 distinct cell and marked-row pairs, including intervals of length seven and 529 binary instances attaining the two-swap bound. It counted the mixed factors independently by minimal signed shape for each $(l,p,r)$ through degree seven. A separate Agda judgment on $D^4\otimes D^4$ verifies the assembly implication with a nonempty prefix and a core of at least three sites. It takes the core term as an input, checks the literal type of the assembled term, and the strict evaluator recovers the original singleton. [Checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_globe_run_refinement.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_globe_run_refinement.py 6 6
python3 -B tools/gray_globe_run_refinement.py 4 4 --agda /tmp/GrayRunRefinement.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayRunRefinement.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 17

**For the threshold construction of Q2, the generic naturality tests can be restricted to a prefix or suffix of one string of parallel variables, together with its cofaces. Every strict cell in each of these test contexts already has a CaTT representative with literally matching faces and the ordinary partial-cylinder generator declarations.** The section assertion holds more generally for *every* partial cylinder on a finite globular pasting arity. The remaining naturality problem prescribes particular face *terms* for the filler. A section chooses one writing of each strict face.

### Which selections reach a threshold

Use Response 10's transition on the old box $D^p\otimes D^n$. Put $j=t-1$, $d=p+j$ and let $x=A_p^*B_j^b$ be its distinguished variable. For every strict cell of dimension at least $d$, the following statements hold, using identity padding when needed.

1. Its support contains $x$ exactly when at least one of its two degree-$d$ rows contains $x$.
2. If its support contains any proper coface of $x$, exactly one of those rows contains $x$. It is the row of sign $b\mathbin{\rm XOR}(p\bmod2)$. If both rows contain $x$, then $x$ is maximal in its support.

Indeed the only possible successor of the site $(p,j)$ is $(p,j+1)$. If the successor occurs, the two signed tags at $(p,j)$ are $(p\bmod2)\mathbin{\rm XOR}e$ on side $e$. If the successor is absent, no generator in the support can have $x$ as a proper face, and the two rows carry the same label at that site whenever it occurs. The rank-ideal condition and the tag formula prove both statements. Typed support of a raw term equals support of its strict interpretation in this presentation. [Signed row formula](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#researcher-response-17) and [total-support lemma, item 16](../answered/naturality-arbitrary-depth-r8-2026-11-18.md)

Consider a substituted coherence head on a globular pasting arity $P$, labelled by old terms. A *slot in degree $d$* means a node of the Batanin tree at depth $d$. Its ordered positions are parallel $d$-variables, and the variables one degree higher between consecutive positions are directed from the earlier position to the later one. Mark a position if its label uses $x$.

At most one slot can contain a marked position. To see this, choose one position independently in every degree-$d$ slot. These choices occur together in a single strict $d$-cell of $P$. In the wedge-of-suspensions description, take the chosen object at each depth-$d$ node, then assemble the branches at successive outer cuts, using identities on shorter branches. Equivalently, start with the full negative $d$-pole and replace its position in each slot by the chosen parallel position. The degree-$d$ chain after labelling is the sum of the top chains of those positions. Two marked choices would give coefficient at least two on $x$, whereas the old tableau has coefficient at most one.

In that one slot the marked positions form a suffix when $x$ is the target-side tag and a prefix when it is the source-side tag. For each connecting $(d+1)$-variable apply assertions 1 and 2 to its label. The indicator of $x$ on its two $d$-faces can change only in the stated direction. The same assertions for higher-dimensional labels show that the *whole* pulled-back selection is exactly the up-closure of the marked positions. In fact, if such a label uses $x$, one of its two $d$-poles uses $x$ and is the label of an iterated face in $P$. The converse is inclusion of typed support. Empty and entire strings are allowed. In a marked-to-marked segment, even a label of arbitrarily high dimension uses no proper coface of $x$.

This argument applies throughout the ordinary substitution recursion. For an inner head compose its labelling with the outer labelling back into the old box. Strict substitution composes, and dependence on the up-closure of $x$ is the same as occurrence of $x$ in the resulting typed support. One can also see stability directly in a globular arity bearing a marked segment. Each strict $d$-row uses at most one position of its slot. In higher cells the portion in that slot traverses consecutive positions in order, so it meets a marked suffix or prefix only if its last or first position does. Pullback along another labelling therefore has the same single-slot description. Consequently the sufficiency proof of Response 11 needs generic fillers only for this class, with the stated compatibility with previously chosen lower actions. In the width-four first-identity test of Response 14, after the $d$ outer levels are split, at most one depth-$d$ branch has a nonglobular cylinder skeleton. All other branches are ordinary globular arities.

The prefix and suffix lengths cannot be bounded using only the fact that the labels form a weak substitution. For example take an old $(d+1)$-column generator $g=A_p^*B_{j+1}^y$. In a chosen depth-$d$ slot send a sequence of positions first to its actual source and then to its actual target. Send all $(d+1)$-positions on the switching segment to $g$ and all stationary ones to identities. At each ancestor of the chosen slot, send the positions up to the chosen child to the corresponding lower source of $g$, and the later positions to its lower target. On every stationary subtree use iterated identities. These assignments are typed even when the faces of $g$ are composite, since they factor through the ordinary disc labelled by $g$. They realize arbitrary lengths on the two sides of a switch, with the mark determined by the indicated tag. This tests possible labellings, without asserting that a particular choice of attaching terms uses them all.

### A section in the prescribed partial-cylinder context

Let $P$ be any finite globular arity, $Y$ any up-closed selection, and $C=P\uparrow Y$ its usual context. For a selected $g$ this context has the copies $g^-,g^+$ and its change variable. Its source starts from $g^-$ and successively composes on the right with each selected target-pole change in increasing cut order. Its target starts from $g^+$ and successively composes on the left with the selected source-pole changes. Use the usual unbiased binary heads and omit a factor at an unselected pole. Write $K_Y$ for the strict partial cylinder. Its differential on a change is $\partial(Ig)=g^+-g^--I(\partial g)$, with $Ih=0$ for an unselected $h$. This is a unital strongly loop-free presentation with these generator faces. We construct the section *in $C$*, keeping every variable at its declared type. [Ordinary partial naturality, §2](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620) and [partial-cylinder atom calculation](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-2026-11-19.md#verification-of-the-researcher-response-2026-11-19)

First take one root branch $P=\Sigma Q$ with endpoints $a,b$. If neither endpoint is selected then $C$ is, up to variable ordering, the suspension of $Q\uparrow Y'$ for the induced selection on $Q$. On the strict side all positive rows desuspend and the only exterior row is $(a,b)$. On the raw side the missing cut-zero factors are omitted and suspension increments all the remaining cuts. Suspending the smaller section gives the assertion on this branch.

If either endpoint is selected, every positive variable of the branch is selected. Start with the full cylinder on $\Sigma Q$ and identify the endpoint copies and collapse the vertical edge at each unselected endpoint. Between the earliest copy of $a$ and the latest copy of $b$, the resulting strict hom is still $I\otimes Q$, via the single-branch diagonal isomorphism of item 6. For example, if $a$ is identified, a path starting on the plus copy lifts by inserting the vertical edge at $a$; one starting on the minus copy requires no insertion there. The analogous end rule applies at $b$. The degree-one rows are thereby recovered uniquely. All higher labels are unchanged. For the equation from degree two to degree one, any discrepancy in the lift would be a chain on the collapsed vertical edges with zero differential. Their endpoint pairs are disjoint, so that chain is zero. The same-time homs, when present, are pure copies. [Full diagonal isomorphism and atom images](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-2026-11-19.md#2-the-strict-hom-decomposition)

Use the section on $I\otimes Q$ with its usual cylinder declarations. In its suspension label a minus-copy variable on $q$ by the minus-copy variable on $\Sigma q$ whiskered on the right by $I b$, omitting that whisker when $b$ is unselected. Label a plus-copy variable by its left whiskering by $I a$, again omitting the absent whisker. Send the suspended change on $q$ to the actual change variable on $\Sigma q$. These formulas are a raw substitution. On the cap variables this follows from the literal faces of binary whiskering, since positive variables of $Q$ have variable poles. On a change declaration, suspension increments all its cuts, and substitution in its two cap variables inserts exactly the occupied cut-zero factors. All its higher pole changes map to the named variables. This recovers the displayed rule for $C$ *literally*. A generator on the plus route when $a$ is identified therefore receives the plus variable itself. The minus route works the same way at an identified $b$. Substituting in the suspended section now gives the required terms on the diagonal. Use ordinary globular terms in the other homs and the interval terms at a selected vertex. This proves the one-branch case including generator pins. [Full cylinder with the usual declarations](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#compatible-choices-on-one-interval)

For a root string of several branches, split first at every unselected interior vertex. Such a seam is a single object. A crossing hom there is the product of the two constituent homs. Indeed its degree-one paths split uniquely at that vertex, and all higher positive rows partition by side since no generator crosses the seam. In the tableau equations the only common contribution of the two pieces is the degree-zero seam object. Conversely their concatenation gives those rows. Compose their assigned terms horizontally with a fixed bracketing and the literal recursive faces of horizontal composition. Cells confined to either piece keep their terms.

It remains to handle a string whose interior root vertices are all selected. A string of length one was treated above. Each positive generator on a longer string has a selected root endpoint in its branch, so this entire piece is a full cylinder with at most its two exterior vertical edges collapsed. For any two distinct spatial endpoints in it, choose the earliest time at an identified starting point and the latest time at an identified ending point. The same path-restoration and differential argument gives a strict isomorphism on this hom from the full-cylinder hom with those endpoints. In particular along the interior the time of a path is nondecreasing, and a time change omitted at an exterior endpoint is uniquely determined by the time of the first or last spatial edge. The equal-time case is a pure panel.

Glue the branch assignments successively at selected interior vertices by the fixed-seam formula of item 7. To check that the formula still applies, restore the outer endpoints on a crossing hom as just described. At a selected seam $z$ the diagonal hom has the same two projections $(l,r)$ as for the full cylinder. Their inverse adds the tableaux and subtracts the common vertical edge $I z$. The condition for positivity is unchanged. At each one-path boundary, either the left projection ends with $I z$ or the right projection starts with it. The corresponding pure-side inclusions are the same full sieve and full cosieve under the hom isomorphism. The mixed case factors through $(p,q)$, determined by the two one-path boundaries alone. In the raw formula the two competing writings at $(p,q)$ represent one path in the partial cylinder with its actual edges; its path arity supplies their 2-comparison. Horizontal composition, whiskering by this comparison and composition at cut one now have exactly the same face equations as in the full-cylinder gluing. All operands come from the two shorter pieces. Every generator lies within one piece and the only shared declarations are those of the seam interval. Thus the construction retains the actual partial-cylinder variables and completes the induction. [Fixed-seam tableau and four term formulas, §§3–6](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r2-2026-11-19.md)

In particular, given a threshold head $c$, the strict cylinder of $\pi c$ has a term in the required context, with the section's own boundary writings. The ordinary naturality judgment requires the two specified expressions built from $c^-,c^+$ and the actions on its actual poles. The single-slot restriction and the section above do not yet identify these expressions with the section's boundaries by compatible CaTT comparisons. That is the remaining step in this route to Q2. Q1 and the unbounded Q2 remain open.

**Verification.** The checker generated the strict cells by closure under composition from the atoms of the actual typed context. Across all 4,139 up-closed selections on the 65 trees with at most five edges, it compared 53,172 cells with the exterior-edge collapse bijection, 479,748 with the product at an unselected seam, and 106,561 with the suspended child. Separately it compared the transferred substitution types with the ordinary change declarations for either choice of a single marked endpoint of $D^1$ through $D^5$. At $D^5$ it recovered all 59 intrinsic diagonal cells on each side by raw terms, with literal faces and generator pins. Agda checked both transferred top declarations and a composite in each orientation. The old $D^5\otimes D^5$ box also passed 89,870 tests of the signed support and transition criterion. Forty weak labellings on branching arities realized the prefix and suffix switches at cuts one through four. [Reproducible checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_partial_cylinder_sections.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_partial_cylinder_sections.py 5 --disc 5 --globe-pair 5 5 --agda /tmp/PartialCylinderSections.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/PartialCylinderSections.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 18

**Every generic head whose two immediate poles are variables has an ordinary partial naturality filler, for all dimensions and all up-closed selections.** The filler has the two prescribed raw terms as its faces and the induced strict cylinder as its value. In particular, temporal compositions of arbitrary length can be used inside the substitution recursion of Response 11 even when the selection reaches the objects. No bound on the dimension of the surrounding terms is required.

Let the head have dimension $n$. The support of either variable pole, including its type, is a single $(n-1)$-globe. In the coherence case the arity must be this globe and its two top poles are the same variable. In the composition case its two boundary arities must each be a single $(n-1)$-globe. Indeed the variable and its type use that entire boundary by the support condition. In the tree for the arity, either signed truncation drops the children at depth $n-1$ and keeps the lower branching. The truncation is a disc, so below depth $n-1$ the tree is a single branch. At that depth it is a path of $l\ge1$ composable $n$-generators. Their outer variables are the poles of the head. This also covers the unary composition head on $D^n$, which has the same poles as the top variable.

### Exact action on the temporal string

First take the unbiased temporal string operation $w$, taking the variable itself for a string of length one. Its immediate poles, and all their poles, are variables. In the context $P\uparrow Y$ use the named changes for these variables. Write $A^-(w),A^+(w)$ for the prescribed two terms obtained from its two copies by the successive whiskerings of Response 17. When the selection is empty the action is omitted. For a unary string with a nonempty selection the required judgment is the actual change declaration. For a string of arrows, change the edges in reverse order. At the current edge keep the earlier minus edges on the left and the later plus edges on the right. Whisker the actual change square by these edges. Its input route has the target vertex change just after the current edge, when present. Its output has the source vertex change just before that edge, when present. An unselected edge has both vertices fixed and its copies coincide. Hence between successive squares one has two writings of exactly the same path. A coherence on its ordinary path arity joins them, also at the initial and final route. The composite has precisely the required endpoints and counts each selected edge square once.

Now let $n\ge2$ and $P=\Sigma Q$ be a temporal string. If neither exterior vertex is marked, suspend the action on the induced selection in $Q$. The two exterior factors are absent. If either exterior vertex is marked, every positive variable is marked. Suspend the full action on the string of $(n-1)$-cells of $Q$, and apply the diagonal substitution of Response 17. For each minus input it inserts the target exterior edge $I b$ on the right if present. For each plus input it inserts the source exterior edge $I a$ on the left if present. Every internal change is sent to the actual variable. The two boundaries thus have exactly the prescribed higher whiskers. The innermost cap, however, is the temporal composite of the individually whiskered inputs.

Consider the minus cap when $b$ is marked. The desired innermost term is $w^- *_0 I b$. The obtained one substitutes $f_j^- *_0 I b$ for the individual arguments of $w$. Both terms have the same literal immediate faces, namely the first source and last target whiskered by $I b$. Here we use $n\ge2$ and the fact that the temporal cut is $n-1$. Both terms use the whole globular arity $P^-\vee_0 I$ and have the same strict value by strict composition. Form the comparison there and substitute the actual variables. Whisker this comparison successively by the named target changes at the occupied cuts $1,\ldots,n-1$. Since the comparison has dimension $n+1$, its two faces are exactly the two corresponding whiskered terms. Use it before the suspended filler. On the plus side use the reverse direction over $I\vee_0 P^+$ and compose after the filler. A missing exterior edge needs no correction. The corrections have identity strict image, since their charts have dimension $n$. The suspended filler uses each selected top change once by induction. These are the only degree-$(n+1)$ generators of the partial cylinder and their differentials are independent, as seen on the plus copies of the top variables. This verifies the strict image as well as the raw type.

### The identity and other writings of the cap operation

On $P=D^{n-1}$, let $w$ be the identity on its variable $z$. For a nonempty selection the change $H_z$ is present. Each prescribed face of the action is a padded identity cap composed at cut $n-1$ with $H_z$. Move the identity through each occupied lower whisker by a comparison over the two-globe pasting arity of that whisker. At a missing whisker simply retain the previous comparison. One reaches the identity on the correspondingly padded base of the cap, which is literally the appropriate face of $H_z$ by its declaration. The top unitor contracts the result to $H_z$. Do this forwards on the minus side and backwards on the plus side. Their composite gives the action, with strict value the identity on $H_z$. This uses the [disk-coordinate degeneracy construction](../answered/canonical-gray-terms-r6-2026-11-20.md#extracting-a-globular-cell-from-a-cylinder) with precisely the occupied factors.

Finally let $c$ be any term on either of the indicated arities with the literal type and full support of $w$. Both have the same strict interpretation. In the string there is just one full $n$-cell between these poles, and in the identity case the arity has height $n-1$. Compare $c^-$ to $w^-$ in the pure minus chart and $w^+$ to $c^+$ in the pure plus chart by full-support coherence. Whisker each comparison through the occupied factors from cut zero upwards. These factors are identical for the two operations since all their poles coincide literally. Compose the resulting comparisons with the action on $w$ at cut $n$. The strict top chain is unchanged and its endpoints are $A^-(c),A^+(c)$. Taking $c$ to be the generic coherence gives the claimed result. Substitution now permits arbitrary arguments with the requisite changes. A formal variable pole specializes to the very argument whose change the caller supplied, so the face equations survive substitution literally.

The remaining threshold tests can therefore be restricted to generic heads with at least one nonvariable immediate pole and with a nonmaximal selected variable in the single marked slot or its up-closure. In particular the preceding cap comparison at an arbitrary lower composition cut would require comparing the *faces* of the separately whiskered composite. Those faces can themselves be different writings. The temporal proof does not supply these comparisons against the previously chosen lower actions. Q1 and unrestricted Q2 remain open.

**Verification.** The checker constructs the actual terms for every nonempty selection on the indicated temporal strings. It infers their two faces, compares them with the independently padded copies, and counts the mixed top variables. It repeats the checks after inserting a top unit into the cap writing, and checks the identity case with arbitrary missing lower factors. For three composable 4-cells there are 42 selections. Agda checks the full 5-term on three composable 4-cells with just the source exterior vertex selected at cut zero. [Term construction and certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_temporal_partial_actions.py)

python3 -B tools/gray_temporal_partial_actions.py 5 2
python3 -B tools/gray_temporal_partial_actions.py 4 3 --agda /tmp/TemporalPartialActions.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/TemporalPartialActions.agda
