# Researcher Response 19

**The ordinary partial naturality action exists for the unbiased composite of every $P=\Sigma^{m-2}B$, where $B$ is any two-dimensional globular pasting arity, for every $m\ge 2$ and every up-closed selection.** Its faces use the temporal actions of Response 18, including when both immediate poles of the composite are nonvariable terms. In particular, the construction applies to composition at cut $m-2$, to whiskering there, and to arbitrary strings of temporal stacks joined there. It is uniform in the number of suspensions, the width of the string and the heights of its stacks. It also applies to any full-support writing with the same literal type as this unbiased operation.

Write $c$ for the unbiased operation and $u,v$ for its source and target. Each of $u,v$ is the unbiased temporal string on the corresponding boundary subarity, included by variables. The two strings have equal length since signed truncation keeps the same root branches of $B$. If the strings are unary, Response 18 already applies. Otherwise both are nonvariable, and all their own proper poles are variables. In particular their changes under substitution by the boundary inclusions are precisely the changes constructed in Response 18.

At $m=2$, prescribe these path actions wherever the selected variables occur, omitting a factor for an unselected pole. The two cylinder endpoints are parallel by the ordinary partial-cylinder typing formula. Their strict values are the faces of the induced strict cylinder of $c$. Boundary lifting in degree three supplies a term between these very endpoints over that cylinder. For clarity, the lifting first writes the strict cell as a composite of atoms. When composing their weak representatives, correct the common one-faces and then the common two-faces by comparisons over identities. The same corrections prescribe the exterior faces. This uses only $\mathrm{RC}(1)$ and $\mathrm{RC}(2)$ in the partial-cylinder context. Its strong Steiner property ensures that the comparisons have strict identity image. [Universal two-dimensional relative coherence and boundary lifting](relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r7.md#researcher-response-7)

### Suspending a filler with temporal boundary actions

Suppose $P=\Sigma Q$ with $m>2$. The operations on $P$ and its two boundary strings are the suspensions of the corresponding unbiased operations on $Q$ and its boundaries. With both exterior vertices fixed, the partial context and the required judgment are the suspensions of the smaller ones, since the cut-zero factors are absent. This also covers selections that omit a whole boundary string. Otherwise all positive variables are marked. In the diagonal hom suspend the full action obtained on $Q$ and make the substitution of Response 17. Call the resulting filler $J$ and its two transferred boundary-string changes $J_u,J_v$. Every positive pole change below $u,v$ is sent to its actual change variable.

Use juxtaposition for the temporal product at cut $m-1$. On the minus side, for $z=c,u,v$, let $C^-(z)$ be the minus copy of $z$ whiskered as a whole by the target exterior edge, if present. Let $D^-(z)$ insert that whisker into each individual input of $z$ before evaluation. Define $C^+,D^+$ with the source exterior edge on the left. Both constructions on a given side commute literally with the faces of $c$. Write $W^-$ for successively whiskering by the common target-pole changes at cuts $1$ through $m-2$, and $W^+$ for the left whiskerings by the common source changes. Use subscripts for the padded terms, as in $C^-_c=W^-(C^-(c))$ and $D^+_c=W^+(D^+(c))$. The type supplied by the induction hypothesis after substitution is
\[
                    J:D^-_c J_v\longrightarrow J_u D^+_c .
\]

For $z=u,v$ let $l_z:C^-(z)\to D^-(z)$ and $r_z:D^+(z)\to C^+(z)$ be exactly the pure-chart comparisons of Response 18. Put $L_z=W^-(l_z)$ and $R_z=W^+(r_z)$. When the respective exterior edge is absent, omit its comparison everywhere. The required operation on the actual temporal face $z$ is $H_z=(L_z J_z)R_z$ with these omissions. The equality is syntactic, including the bracketing: it is how the temporal face operation was constructed before the boundary inclusion.

The two additional comparisons needed for the lift are
\[
        C^-_c L_v \longrightarrow L_u D^-_c ,
        \qquad
        D^+_c R_v \longrightarrow R_u C^+_c .
\]
For the first, work initially on the single globular chart $P^-\vee_0 I$. The two composites $C^-(c)l_v$ and $l_uD^-(c)$ are literally parallel from $C^-(u)$ to $D^-(v)$. The comparisons $l_u,l_v$ are images of their boundary charts in this chart. Both composites have full support and the same strict value, since the strict whisker distributes over the entire pasting and each boundary comparison has identity image. A coherence between them in this chart has identity image by dimension. The analogous construction on $I\vee_0P^+$ starts at $D^+(u)$ and ends at $C^+(v)$.

Apply $W^-$ or $W^+$ to that comparison. To obtain exactly the displayed products one also needs to distribute $W$ over a temporal pair. For one fixed cut-$k$ whisker with $k<m-1$, take two composable $m$-globes at cut $m-1$ and glue a $(k+1)$-globe on the indicated side at cut $k$. This is a globular pasting arity. Whiskering the pair as a whole and multiplying its two whiskered inputs have literally the same $(m-1)$-faces by the binary head equations, and full support. The two directed coherences there give both distributors after substitution. For a list of whiskers, whisker the preceding distributor through the next factor and compose with that factor's distributor. Thus one can collect the two factors on the source side of the pure-chart comparison and distribute them on its target side, even when the actual whiskers have nonglobular attaching terms.

Start at $C^-_c H_v$. Use the first square to move $L_v$ to $L_u$, retaining $J_v,R_v$ to its right. Apply $J$ between $L_u$ and $R_v$. Then use the second square to move $R_v$ to $R_u$, retaining $L_u,J_u$ on its left. At every junction use a coherence on the temporal string of the displayed factors to implement the precise bracketing. This ends at $H_u C^+_c$. With an exterior edge absent the corresponding move is omitted. Every added comparison and reassociation is strictly an identity, so the only top contribution is the one from $J$. By induction and the diagonal atom formula this is precisely one copy of the change of each selected top generator. These facts give the required strict cell and the literal partial naturality type. Finally a different full-support cap writing with this same type can be compared to $c$ on each pure copy of $P$. Whisker those comparisons through the occupied factors, including the constructed $H_u,H_v$ at the last cut, and compose with the action just constructed.

### Replacing both face terms of a known operation

The same two-square paste gives a transport rule on *any* globular arity $P$. Suppose an $m$-term $w:u\to v$ has a partial action, and replace each $z=u,v$ by a term $z'$ with its literal type and strict value. Use a globular subarity containing the support of $z$ and $z'$ for its pure comparisons, for example the appropriate signed boundary for a composition head. Choose $p_z:z'\to z$ and $q_z:z\to z'$ there over strict identities. For an active face prescribe its new action as the temporal composite of the padded minus copy of $p_z$, the old $H_z$, and the padded plus copy of $q_z$, in that order. Here padding runs over all occupied cuts through $m-2$ using the old proper-pole actions. A specified action with an invertible identity-image comparison to this composite works as well. Then **any $c:u'\to v'$ on $P$ with the strict value of $w$ has an action with these new faces**. In particular the preceding result accepts simultaneous changes of both temporal boundary writings with the face actions chosen by the last construction of Response 18.

Indeed the minus square before padding compares $c p_v$ with $p_u w$ on $P$; the plus square compares $w q_v$ with $q_u c$ there. Their two routes are parallel and have the same strict value. Globular boundary lifting gives both squares over identities, also when the routes occupy a proper subarity. Substitution into the copies and the same two-factor distributors, now starting at cut zero, give precisely the two squares needed to paste around $H_w$. If alternative face actions came with comparisons, whisker those at the beginning and end.

If a face $z$ is inactive then all of its proper poles are fixed. Use the auxiliary identity $1_z$ on that side of the reference action, inserting a top unitor to match its original type. The corresponding temporary change on $z'$ is $(p_z 1_z)q_z$. Both copies of the whole support agree on this inactive face. Comparing this loop with $1_{z'}$ over the unmarked face arity and using the top unitor converts the pasted judgment back to the required expression with this factor omitted. One may take the comparisons on the actual unmarked support: for a full generic head its face support is its whole arity or the corresponding signed boundary. This gives both one-sided omission cases and preserves the strict image. Thus replacements at this layer require no relative-coherence judgment in the mixed context. Compatibility with an action assigned by some other term recursion still requires the stated comparison of face actions.

This discharges threshold tests involving a genuine lower composition cut and composite immediate faces. In a tree with branching three or more levels below the top, the boundary-string formula $H_z=(L_z J_z)R_z$ is no longer supplied by Response 18. Applying the two squares alone does not compare the resulting changes on those higher faces. The unrestricted globe-pair and cube assertions in Q2 and the general assertion in Q1 remain open.

**Verification.** For all 21 nonempty selections on the two-dimensional arity of a 2-cell followed by an arrow, the checker reconstructs the strict mixed 3-cell from the atoms of the partial cylinder, corrects its one- and two-faces, and evaluates the result back to the full strict tableau. The suspension check compares both transported temporal face actions with the independent term construction of Response 18. It verifies both one-ended choices and the full choice and counts the top variables. A separate judgment changes both temporal poles, inserting a left unit into one writing and a right unit into the other. Agda checked the concrete 4-term after one suspension, including the constructed base action. A second certificate takes the smaller judgment as a hypothesis and checks the resulting 5-term at its prescribed type. # Researcher Response 20

**Every unbiased operation on every finite globular pasting arity admits the ordinary partial naturality action for selections generated in one degree.** The dimension of the operation, the depth of the marks, and the branching of the arity are unrestricted. The choices have the recursively prescribed face actions and the induced strict cylinder as their value. This includes all the selections in Response 17. For arbitrary heads, the threshold implication of Response 11 now needs additional generic actions only for heads whose dimension exceeds the height of their arity. Their strict values are identities. This is a sufficiency reduction for Q2.

### Raising an existing action at its top slots

Let $S$ be a globular arity of height $r$, with an up-closed temporal selection $Y$. Expand any nonempty set $Z$ of its $r$-variables into strings of $(r+1)$-variables, obtaining $P$. The word *spatial* below denotes this second direction, to distinguish it from the original change on $Y$. An expanded slot $x$ has positions $x_0,\ldots,x_l$ and arrows between them. Keep the old temporal marks on $P$: in an expanded slot mark all its positions and arrows if $x\in Y$, and none otherwise. String lengths may differ and slots outside $Z$ stay fixed. Write $i_0,i_1:S\to P$ for the two inclusions on variables.

Take a full $r$-term $w$ on $S$ with an ordinary temporal action $h=H_w$ of the prescribed strict value. Its proper-pole actions must be local to their terms, as in the ordinary substitution recursion. Put $f_x$ equal to the unbiased temporal composite of the spatial string at $x$, taking its variable for a one-arrow string. Let $D w$ be the depth-zero spatial action of $w$ at $Z$ evaluated on these $f_x$. It goes from $w[i_0]$ to $w[i_1]$. Then **$D w$ has a temporal action using the two variable-inclusion instances of the given $h$ on its immediate poles**. In particular this construction works for a previously chosen $h$ with arbitrarily deep temporal marks in $w$.

To see this, put $G=S\uparrow Y$. In $G$ spatially mark the two temporal copies $x^-,x^+$ and their change $H_x$ for $x\in Z\cap Y$. Mark just the single copy for $x\in Z\setminus Y$. This set is up-closed. No old generator has a top slot in its type. Among temporal declarations, only the change of that slot has either top copy in its type. The lowest spatial marks now have dimension $r$ and $h$ has dimension $r+1$. Thus spatial naturality of $h$ exists by the depth-one theorem, regardless of how $h$ was constructed. Oppose dimension $r+2$ in this last judgment. This reverses the new highest arrows and their types and leaves all terms of dimension at most $r+1$ intact. This is the top-dimensional opposition used to raise the inputs of ordinary cylinder composition. [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorems 3.1 and 4.2 and the depth-zero head in §3.2(6)](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

Here is the substitution from this opposed context into the actual temporal cylinder on $P$, written as labels. The two spatial copies of the old data are their $i_0,i_1$ instances. Label the spatial change of a temporal cap copy of $x$ by the same temporal copy of $f_x$. For a doubly marked $x$ label the opposed spatial change of $H_x$ by the temporal action of $f_x$ from Response 18. That action is available on the entire string, including its possibly unselected proper poles.

For the typing calculation write $A^-(z),A^+(z)$ for the two temporal caps with their lower occupied whiskers. Spatial depth-zero action on $A^e(w)$ is literally $A^e(D w)$ with the old whiskers, before the additional factor at cut $r$. Indeed the proper-pole changes occurring in those whiskers contain no spatially marked variable by locality and rank. At a single whisker the spatial mark pulls back to just the maximal position of the moving operand. The depth-zero head is the coherence on the arity with this position raised. This is the two-globe arity of the whiskered higher operand, and its two generic faces are the original binary head on the two copies. It is therefore exactly the unbiased higher whisker. Iterating handles all occupied cuts, including $r-1$ when the stationary operand has dimension $r$. On the pure cap the equality follows from substitution for depth-zero actions under the variable inclusions. The same calculation applies with $w=x$.

Consequently the opposed declaration at $H_x$ is mapped to
\[
 A^-(f_x)*_r H_{x_l}\ \longrightarrow\ H_{x_0} *_r A^+(f_x).
\]
This is precisely the type of the supplied string action. The opposed action on $h$ itself is then mapped to
\[
 A^-(D w)*_r h[i_1]\ \longrightarrow\ h[i_0] *_r A^+(D w).
\]
These are raw type equations. In particular a spatial slot outside $Y$ contributes the single label $f_x$ and requires no mixed label. If $Y$ is empty throughout, temporal actions are omitted.

The strict spatial top chain of $D w$ contains every expanded arrow once. Before the string substitution this follows from the two caps of the depth-zero action: the two copies of each marked top slot occur once in the respective caps, and only its spatial change has a differential on that copy. Substituting the strings replaces these changes by their full top chains. In $P\uparrow Y$ the only generators of degree $r+2$ are the temporal changes of the marked spatial arrows. Their differentials are independent, detected on the plus copies of the individual arrows. The displayed output therefore has the induced strict cylinder value: the caps and lower changes have the required strict images, and the differential determines the remaining top chain. This also proves the assertion when every expanded arrow is stationary in time.

### All unbiased operations at the threshold marks

For a head on an arity of height $m$, assume its selection is the up-closure of positions of degree $d$. If $m\le d+1$, use depth-zero or depth-one naturality, with its usual choices on the lower poles. For $m>d+1$ take $r=m-1$ and identify all $r$-positions in each $r$-slot to form the common truncation $S$. Its two embeddings are the signed boundary inclusions. Every position in each such string has the same proper $d$-poles, since $r>d$. Hence the selection in $P$ is exactly the expansion of the selection on $S$ described above. No assumption about the number of marked $d$-slots is needed.

Apply the construction to the unbiased $w$ of $S$ and its inductively chosen temporal action. The unbiased $c$ of $P$ and $D w$ have the same literal type: each signed boundary of $c$ is $w$ on that variable inclusion. Both have the strict full cell of $P$ as value. In each pure copy of $P$, compare the two cap writings in the required direction by a full-support coherence. Whisker the comparisons successively by the occupied proper-pole changes, finishing with the already chosen copies of $H_w$ at cut $r$. Compose with the action just built. This gives exactly the action of $c$ at the prescribed faces, with identity-image adjustments. A variable keeps its declared change. Choosing the operations on each generic arity and using its boundary inclusions gives one recursive choice. In particular, the cut of a binary composition or whiskering may be arbitrarily far below its output degree. Terms formed by substituting such unbiased heads and identities can use the same construction whenever their pulled-back marks have the rank property. Identity on a given changed term uses the disk-coordinate degeneracy with its occupied factors, as in Response 18.

### Changing the writings of several spatial strings at once

For each expanded slot choose two terms $f_x^0,f_x^1$ on its string with the literal faces and strict value of the full string composite. Take $f_x^0$ to be the unbiased writing. In an active slot take its change from Response 18 and choose the change of $f_x^1$ by the pure cap corrections of that response. On $P$ the two evaluations $(D w)[f^0]$ and $(D w)[f^1]$ have the same faces $w[i_0],w[i_1]$. Here $D w$ before evaluation is computed on the template with a single spatial arrow $F_x$ at each expanded slot. Give each evaluation the substitution instance of the temporal action on that template. **Every pure comparison between these two evaluations admits a temporal action with those specified evaluation actions on its faces.**

Indeed, expand the top slots $F_x$ of the template once more, each by a single arrow $E_x:F_x^0\to F_x^1$. Apply the raising construction to $D w$ and its supplied temporal action. This produces a comparison and its action before any string labels are substituted. To label $E_x$, choose a pure comparison $e_x:f_x^0\to f_x^1$ on the actual string. For an active slot its temporal change must use exactly the assigned changes of $f_x^0$ and $f_x^1$. Obtain this judgment from the disk identity action on $f_x^0$ by the face replacement of Response 19, retaining its source and replacing its target. On the retained side omit both adjustments, or use the top unitors to identify the adjusted action with the given one. The target adjustments are precisely the pure cap corrections used to assign the action of $f_x^1$. In a stationary slot only the pure comparison is needed. These labels agree on shared proper faces because the higher expansion fixes those faces. They therefore give a substitution, and the two evaluations of the first template action are literally the two required boundary actions of the resulting comparison. Its strict image is an identity since the second spatial comparison has degree $r+2$ in the arity $P$ of height $r+1$. Finally any other pure comparison with these same raw faces can replace it by the two pure cap corrections, whiskered through all occupied cuts. This settles thin comparisons from simultaneous changes of string writings even at unbounded depth. It uses the specified cap-corrected actions on the individual strings.

### Eliminating composition heads with arbitrary boundary writings

Let $c$ now be a generic $m$-head on $P$ of height $m$, with $m>d+1$. By the composition support condition its two poles live on the two copies of $S$. Pull them back to terms $u,v$ on $S$. They have full support and literally the same type: their common proper poles have rank below $r$ and the two inclusions coincide there. In $S$ choose
\[
                         b=\mathsf{coh}_{S,\,u\to v}[\mathrm{id}].
\]
Its strict image is an identity on the full $r$-cell. Suppose the temporal action of this one thin head is available on $S$ with the already chosen actions of $u$ and $v$. If $u=v$ the identity on $u$ suffices and no extra head is needed.

Raise $v$ spatially using $H_v$ as above. On $P$ the term $b[i_0]*_r Dv$ has the exact two poles of $c$ and its strict full top chain. To give this product its temporal action, use the binary temporal operation of Response 18 on the action of $b[i_0]$ and the action of $Dv$. Their common change is literally $H_v[i_0]$. On their outer poles the result uses $H_u[i_0]$ and $H_v[i_1]$, just as the original head requires. Finally switch the cap writing to $c$ by the two pure comparisons and whisker them through those same proper-pole changes. This step takes place entirely over the generic arity, before substituting any labels in a globe pair.

Thus at dimension $m$ one may first handle heads of arity height less than $m$, using the already assigned actions in smaller dimension, and then obtain *all* height-$m$ heads. In the first group a strict $m$-cell is an identity. Its induced strict temporal cylinder is the disk-coordinate identity on the cylinder of its immediate pole. The outstanding tests compare the two specified raw $(m)$-terms over this strict value. Literal endomorphism heads can use the identity action followed by pure cap corrections. For distinct pole writings the comparison must still agree with the actions computed on those poles by substitution. Response 17 restricts their selections to one slot at the threshold. A proof of these thin-head comparisons in all dimensions would finish the globe-pair implication. This response discharges the composition heads and leaves those comparisons, Q1 and unrestricted Q2 open.

**Verification.** The checker constructs the opposed spatial domain, labels every mixed variable by an actual term, and checks the substitution entry by entry. It independently constructs the desired type on the expanded arity and compares it with the substituted type of the depth-one operation. Cases include a spatially moving but temporally fixed top slot, a fixed lower whisker on another branch, two incident stacks of lengths two and three, and both non-unbiased cap writings and changed pole writings. Agda checks the substitution implication with the smaller theorem's term quantified over its own domain. It also checks concrete outputs with a shared top slot and with disjoint moving leaves. In the latter case the thin bridge, temporal composition and both pure cap changes are actual terms. [Certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_top_slot_promotion.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_top_slot_promotion.py 4
python3 -B tools/gray_top_slot_promotion.py 3 --agda /tmp/TopSlotPromotion.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/TopSlotPromotion.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 21

**At each rank, the thin-head test of Response 20 reduces to one comparison per elementary substitution. If the pure support has the same height as the terms being compared, every elementary substitution whose lower labels are variables passes the test.** This includes arbitrary subdivisions of several top strings, empty blocks, and strings with different recursive bracketings. The actions on the bracketings are the ones obtained by substituting the actions of their arguments. The general globe-pair construction still needs the elementary substitutions specified below.

### Comparing a term with a single writing on its support

Fix the ordinary actions through degree $k$, with their strict values and substitution recursion. In a pure globular arity let $t$ be a $k$-term. Its strict cell has a support subarity $P_t$. Indeed, between objects $a<b$ of a wedge of suspensions the strict hom is the product of the branches between them. Take the support of each coordinate recursively, suspend, and join them in order. An identity at an object uses its point, and identity padding in higher degree preserves the support. This proves the assertion by tree induction, including cells with branches of unequal height. By total support it also gives the raw typed support of $t$.

Choose $N(t)$ on this subarity using only the strict cell and the literal type of $t$. Use the top variable on a disk when it has that type. For an identity value with literally equal poles use the identity on that pole. Otherwise use the single coherence at the indicated type over $P_t$ and include it. If $P_t$ has height $k$, the two poles use its signed boundaries. At smaller height both poles use the full support. These are exactly the two CaTT support conditions. Its strict value is fixed by those poles and the full top chain. This choice commutes with inclusions of subarities. In particular two parallel terms with the same strict value have the very same $N$.

For such $u,v$ say a pure comparison $p:u\to v$ *has an action* if it has an ordinary action at the already assigned $H_u,H_v$. Restrict to their common support of height at most $k$. If it is active both ends are active. All the pure comparisons under discussion have identity strict value. Existence for one gives existence for every writing with the same endpoints by the pure cap correction of Response 18. The identity comparison has its disk degeneracy action. Composable comparisons with actions compose by the temporal binary operation at their actual common change.

This relation also reverses, even when $H_u$ is noninvertible. Write $F,G$ for the endpoint copies padded through every occupied cut below $k$, and write juxtaposition for cut $k$. A forward square has type $F(p)H_v\to H_uG(p)$. It has a reverse cell since its degree $k+2$ exceeds the height of the mixed context. Choose any pure $q:v\to u$. Starting from $F(q)H_u$, insert $G(p)G(q)$ on the right, apply the reverse cell to $H_uG(p)$, and contract $F(q)F(p)$ on the left. The result ends at $H_vG(q)$. For each insertion or contraction, collect the two padded factors by the distributors of Response 19, use a comparison of the pure loop with the identity on its actual pole, and move that identity through the lower whiskers. The top unitor gives the declared endpoint. Use the reversed comparisons for insertion. Rebracketings live on the temporal string of the displayed factors. Thus this produces an action for $q$ without inverting either vertical side. [Invertibility criterion for free weak categories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.12127)

Here is the elementary test. Write $t=h[\sigma]$ for a generic $k$-head on $Q$ evaluated in a pure arity. Require each degree-$k$ entry $z$ of $\sigma$ to be $N(z)$. Entries of smaller degree may be arbitrary. For every such substitution test **one** comparison
\[
                         h[\sigma]\longrightarrow N(h[\sigma])
\]
with the action on its source obtained by ordinary substitution. One can restrict to the actual support of the result and the inherited selection of Response 17.

To prove sufficiency, take an unrestricted $h[\sigma]$. Replace each degree-$k$ argument $z$ by $N(z)$, obtaining $\bar\sigma$. Its declaration still holds literally. By induction on the raw input expressions, each replacement comparison is already available with its action wherever active. When $Q$ has height $k$, apply Response 20's raising lemma to $h,H_h$ at just the top slots being changed, with one spatial arrow per slot. Label its ends by $\sigma,\bar\sigma$, its spatial arrows by the replacement comparisons, and its mixed arrows by their supplied actions. The declarations are those of the ordinary actions on the comparisons, exactly as in the opposed substitution of Response 20. A stationary temporal slot takes only its pure comparison. The resulting pure comparison goes from $h[\sigma]$ to $h[\bar\sigma]$, with the two actual substitution instances of $H_h$ as its boundary actions. The two ends retain their literal lower poles and strict value. If $Q$ has no degree-$k$ slots there is no replacement step. Apply the elementary test at the root and compose. Recursion descends only into proper input expressions, regardless of the size of their normal writings. For an arbitrary requested $u\to v$, compose the normalization of $u$ with the reverse of the normalization of $v$, then change the writing to the requested head. Necessity follows by taking the comparison in the test itself on the support subarity. This proves the equivalence for the thin-head stage in the globe-pair argument.

### Recursive composition of squares along a temporal string

There is a choice of the generic temporal string actions with the following extra property. Let $a_i:x_{i-1}\to x_i$ be a string of $k$-cells. Form words from the $a_i$ by composition at cut $k-1$ and identities at the positions. Allow any arity of temporal composition. For two bracketings of the same path, including insertions of units, **every pure comparison between them has an action at the recursively evaluated actions of the words**. It suffices to prove this over the generic string. Substitution then allows any labelling with the required changes.

For $k\ge2$ fix the common $(k-2)$-poles and use the bicategory whose objects are $(k-1)$-terms between them, arrows are $k$-terms, and 2-cells are $(k+1)$-terms modulo invertible $(k+2)$-comparison with fixed boundary. For $k=1$ use the same construction starting with objects. Binary composition, unit, associator and unitors are the disk and string operations. They give a bicategory: the interchange law lives on the horizontal pasting of two vertical strings, and naturality of the associator, the pentagon and the triangle live on their globular path arities. The two parallel writings of each law lift the same strict cell there, so globular boundary lifting supplies the comparison before substitution. The same argument makes composition respect the quotient. A chain of equalities in this quotient supplies an actual comparison by composing witnesses and their inverses.

In this paragraph let $F,G$ pad only through cut $k-2$. They are pseudofunctors from the pure bicategory to the mixed one. For a single fixed lower whisker, its composition constraint distributes it over a pair of consecutive arrows. Its unit constraint compares the whiskered identity with the identity of the whiskered object. Each has both directions on the globular chart obtained by gluing the whisker disk to the corresponding pure arity. For preservation of vertical composition use a vertical pair of 2-cells in that chart. For naturality of the composition constraint use a horizontal pair of 2-cells. For the associativity constraint use a three-arrow path, and for the two unit laws use a single arrow. In each case the gluing cut is strictly below the temporal cut, the two routes have literally the same boundary after inserting the bicategory associators and unitors, and strict whiskering gives equal strict values. Globular boundary lifting therefore proves each equality in the quotient. Compose the pseudofunctors for successive occupied whiskers. This treats even a later whisker whose actual attaching term is composite.

Each generator change is now a square $F(a_i)H_{x_i}\Rightarrow H_{x_{i-1}}G(a_i)$, with horizontal composition at cut $k-1$. At an inactive position insert a temporary identity by unitors. An inactive arrow has the unit square between the two writings with those identities. Define the binary operation by pasting the right square and then the left one, using the composition constraints of $F,G$ at the ends. Define the identity operation by the unit constraints and unitors. At the exterior inactive positions undo the temporary unitors to obtain the literal ordinary type. The same insertions at an interior seam cancel by the bicategory unit laws. Choose representatives for these formulas on the generic charts before substitution. Their strict values are the ordinary cylinder values, since pasting uses each active input change once and its structural adjustments have identity value. For an unbiased operation of greater arity use one fixed binary bracketing and change its cap to the unbiased writing by the two pure comparisons. Thus the construction defines raw generic terms and their substitution instances.

For the associator between three arrows, expand both recursive pastes. The composition constraints for $F$ and $G$ obey the pseudofunctor associativity law. After applying that law at the two ends, both routes paste the same three squares, in the same right-to-left order, with the associators of the bicategory. They agree by bicategorical pasting. The unit constraints and the triangle give the corresponding statement for inserting a unit. Whiskering these equalities and composing them proves the assertion for any sequence of rebracketings. In a pure temporal string any other comparison between the resulting words agrees with this one in the pure bicategory by globular boundary lifting. The two pseudofunctors preserve that equality. With $p:u\to v$ the pure comparison, the equality so obtained has sides $(F(p)*_{k-1}H_{x_l})H_v$ and $H_u(H_{x_0}*_{k-1}G(p))$, with the indicated exterior omissions. The outer products here are at cut $k$. These are exactly the raw endpoints required for its action. [Johnson–Yau, bicategorical pasting theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/1910.01220)

### Subdividing and collapsing several top strings

Consider an elementary test on its full support $P$ of height $k$, with every label below $k$ a variable. Then $Q$ also has height $k$. A top label lies between variable $(k-1)$-positions. Its support is either a temporal string of $k$-cells or one disk of height $k-1$: the common truncation of its support is the disk of its variable pole, so the only possible branching is in the top string. Its normal writing is the full string composite, its single generator, or the identity on its position. Replace each top arrow of $Q$ by that string, identifying its ends for an empty block. The resulting globular tree maps into $P$ entirely by variables. Such a map is injective because the order generated by $s(x)<x<t(x)$ on the positions of a globular tree is strict and total, and the target has the same property. Its image is the full support of the term. Thus it identifies the refined tree with $P$. In particular $P$ and $Q$ have a common signed truncation $S$ in degree $k-1$, even if whole strings vanish. The degree-one interpretation uses the vertices and edges of a path.

For threshold degree $d<k-1$, choose the following version of the construction in Response 20. In $S$ give **every** top slot one formal spatial arrow to obtain a single template. Evaluate it on a given arity by its full string in that slot, using a weak identity when the whole string is empty. The temporal selection is constant on each string because its proper $d$-poles agree. Thus the template has a temporal labelling, including at a collapsed slot by the disk degeneracy. The two old signed boundary embeddings are exactly the ends of this labelling.

For a generic head with signed boundary writings $u,v$ on $S$, build $b[i_0]*_{k-1}Dv$ and its action once on the template, with $b:u\to v$ and its action from the preceding rank of the thin-head test (degree $k$). If $u=v$ use $Dv$ alone. On each arity take the evaluation of this reference action and then the two pure cap corrections to the actual head. In the case of an unbiased temporal string the reference on its template is just its single arrow, so take the temporal operation constructed above. A variable keeps its declared action. On a disk a generic coherence at the literal type of its top variable may take a cap correction around that variable. These conventions also handle the corresponding unary substitutions. Shallow selections at depth at most two admit prescribed-boundary lifting by Response 13.

Apply this choice separately to $h$ on $Q$ and its normal writing on $P$. They have identical boundary data on $S$. After the substitution into $P$, the two reference terms are instances of the very same term on the template. In each slot the first labelling uses the composite of the block composites, and the second uses the full flat string. The first change is the *recursive* action at these blocks. The temporal-string result gives an action on a comparison to the second labelling in every active slot, including empty blocks. Apply the simultaneous top-slot replacement proved above to the reference term on the template. This gives a comparison of the two reference evaluations at their specified actions. Finally apply the two-face replacement rule of Response 19. Its face adjustments are precisely the pure cap corrections used to define the two generic actions, including the substituted corrections from $Q$. It therefore supplies the comparison at the actual two writings. This proves the elementary test in this case.

At smaller height there is also a class of automatic tests. If the generic head is literally an endomorphism on a strict identity value, choose its action by changing the cap of the disk degeneracy on its pole, as in Response 18. After any substitution the normal writing is the identity on that substituted pole. Its disk action is the substitution instance of the very same degeneracy. Consequently the action on the original writing is a pure cap correction around the action on its normal writing. Apply Response 19's two-face replacement to the identity comparison on the normal writing, changing only its source. This supplies the elementary test with arbitrary lower labels. This argument uses equality of the two poles before substitution.

If $Q$ itself has height below $k$ and every lower label is a variable, the entire labelling is by variables. The order argument identifies $Q$ with its image. The two writings then coincide on their support, except for the identity convention just treated. If $Q$ has height $k$ and its image has smaller height with variable lower labels, the $(k-1)$-positions still map to variables, so the support has height exactly $k-1$. Every top string collapses in this case. Thus the remaining elementary tests either have a nonvariable label **below** rank $k$, or collapse every top string onto the common truncation. In particular the second case has excess exactly one for the term being normalized. Neither class is settled in general, and Q1 and the unrestricted alternatives of Q2 remain open.

**Verification.** The first checker normalizes unequal nested terms to the same raw writing, retains differently written poles on a higher identity value, and constructs the reverse square along a separately written reverse comparison. The vertical side uses nonidentity generators in a cylinder marked at an exterior vertex. The second checker constructs a comparison on three moving squares using their actual two binary evaluation orders. It checks the associator against the distributor for a left or right whisker at every lower cut. A separate substitution subdivides one top string with block lengths $(0,2,1)$ and collapses another with lengths $(0,0)$, checking the temporal declaration of every entry. Agda checks the rank-three reverse square, the lower-whisker comparison and the mixed substitution. [Normalization and reversal checker](../../../tools/gray_thin_substitution.py) · [Temporal reassociation and subdivision checker](../../../tools/gray_temporal_reassociation.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_thin_substitution.py 4
python3 -B tools/gray_temporal_reassociation.py 4
python3 -B tools/gray_thin_substitution.py 3 --agda /tmp/ThinNormalizationHigher.agda
python3 -B tools/gray_temporal_reassociation.py 3 --agda /tmp/TemporalFold.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/ThinNormalizationHigher.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/TemporalFold.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 22

**The complete collapse in Response 21 passes the elementary substitution test in every dimension.** Moreover, a top argument labelled by an identity on *any* term can be eliminated, even if the other arguments have nonvariable lower boundaries. Thus every remaining test has a nonvariable lower label and can be taken to have no identity among its top labels. The point is to prove a unit law for the action of Response 20 on the *already chosen* temporal change. An identity value for the collapsed strict cell alone would not give this law.

### A unit law at spatial depth one

Let $r$ be the lowest spatially marked rank. Work through rank $r+1$ in a context $C$. Write $D_z$ for the ordinary depth-zero action on an active $r$-term $z$. For a term $f:s\to t$ of rank $r+1$, oppose rank $r+2$ in its ordinary spatial action, as in Response 20. Denote the result by $J_f$. Give the two spatial copies the same labelling in a context $G$ of height at most $r+1$. Write $\ast$ for cut $r$ and $\cdot$ for cut $r+1$, in order of traversal. The resulting square has type
\[
                    J_f:D_s\ast f\longrightarrow f\ast D_t .
\]
Here and below $f$ in a labelled formula stands for its common cap image. An inactive pole omits its factor. Use the bicategory of Response 21 with $r$-terms as objects and $(r+2)$-terms modulo invertible $(r+3)$-comparison as 2-cells.

At each marked $r$-variable $x$ give a contraction $e_x:D_x\Rightarrow 1_x$. These extend to contractions $e_z$ on active $r$-terms by the construction below. Suppose that for each marked $(r+1)$-variable $f$, the labelled square obeys
\[
 (e_s\ast f)\cdot\lambda_f
       = J_f\cdot(f\ast e_t)\cdot\rho_f .                       \tag{1}
\]
The unitors have direction towards $f$. Omit both the contraction and its unitor on an inactive side. Then **(1) holds for every active $(r+1)$-term**, including a coherence with nonvariable immediate poles.

First consider an $r$-term $z$ on a pure pasting arity $B$ with a selection of top slots. Give those slots identity changes. In $B$ choose $U_z:D_z(1)\Rightarrow1_z$ by globular boundary lifting. Both sides are loops over the same strict identity since $B$ has height $r$ on the support in question. Their comparison is unique modulo the next comparison there. For labels with contractions $e_i$, vary the change labels in the term $D_z$ by these $e_i$. This is again depth zero, now in rank $r+1$. Follow the result by the labelled $U_z$ to obtain $e_z$. On arbitrary raw terms use this formula recursively at their generic heads. At a variable use the supplied contraction.

This prescription commutes with substitution in the bicategory. Varying the change labels of $D_z$ respects a composite of two changes up to comparison: at every changing leaf graft two vertically composable $(r+2)$-cells. Both orders of evaluation on this globular tree have the same faces and strict value, so a comparison exists before labelling. For a substitution between pure arities, separate the contractions of the inner arguments into their variations of the individual change labels followed by their pure $U$'s. The first parts combine to the twice-differentiated substituted term by the ordinary substitution equation, applied once in each of the two top degrees. The remaining parts, including the outer $U$, are both comparisons from the action at identities to the identity on the substituted term in the pure source arity. They agree in the quotient there. Substituting the actual data gives the equation for one composite of generic heads. Induction on the outer expression gives it for arbitrary labellings, including labellings in $G$. The active slots at each step are exactly the pullbacks of the variable selection. [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorem 3.1 and Lemma 3.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620)

Now write $f=c[\sigma]$ with generic head $c$ on $Q$. Let $K$ be the opposed depth-one spatial cylinder of $Q$ at its pulled-back marks. Compare two labellings of $K$. The first evaluates the spatial actions recursively on the arguments of $\sigma$. For the second, first collapse $K$ into pure $Q$: both endpoint copies of an argument become that argument, a rank-$r$ change becomes its identity, and the opposed square on a rank-$(r+1)$ variable becomes the left unitor followed by the reversed right unitor, with missing factors omitted. Then apply $\sigma$. Call these labellings $a,b$.

In $K$ mark **only the change variables**, retaining both sets of endpoint copies as fixed. This is up-closed, and its lowest possible rank is $r+1$. Give the variable on a rank-$r$ change its $e_{\sigma x}$ as the comparison from $a$ to $b$. At the square on a rank-$(r+1)$ argument $y$, the ordinary change declaration asks to compare
\[
     J_{\sigma y}\cdot(\sigma y\ast e_{\sigma t y})
       \quad\text{with}\quad
     (e_{\sigma s y}\ast\sigma y)\cdot J_y[b].
\]
These are precisely (1) for that proper argument after cancelling the common right unitor. Indeed changing the moving operand in the cap whisker acts by the higher whisker on its change, by the depth-zero graft formula. The induction hypothesis supplies an actual comparison since equality in this quotient is witnessed by an invertible cell. If neither pole moves, the declaration directly compares the two squares. This labels the second cylinder.

Apply ordinary depth-one naturality in this second cylinder to the term $J_c$ of rank $r+2$. This step is available for the opposed term just as for any term at that rank. It relates $J_c[a]$ and $J_c[b]$ with the two variations of $D_s,D_t$ on their boundaries, leaving the cap copies of $c$ fixed. For $J_c[b]$, equation (1) with the pure $U_s,U_t$ already holds before applying $\sigma$: the two displayed routes have the same literal endpoints after the unitors, and the same strict identity on $c$ in pure $Q$. Globular boundary lifting compares them. Whisker and compose the two equalities. The preceding substitution calculation identifies the variation on each pole followed by its pure $U$ with exactly $e_{s[\sigma]}$ or $e_{t[\sigma]}$. Ordinary naturality commutes with substitution, and opposition in the highest degree commutes with the opposed labelling. Thus $J_c[a]$ is the prescribed $J_f$. This proves (1) by raw term induction. Every use of spatial naturality here has depth at most one. The temporal marks to which the lemma will be applied can begin arbitrarily far below $r$.

### Collapsing the promotion of an existing temporal action

Use the common truncation $S$ of Response 21, of height $r=k-1$, with its temporal context $G=S\uparrow Y$. Collapse each formal spatial arrow of the template to the identity on its slot. In the opposed spatial cylinder of $G$, the change of an endpoint copy of a spatial slot is now a weak identity. The square on its temporal change $H_x$ is the temporal disk action on $1_x$. Write $W^-,W^+$ for the two respective endpoint embeddings and their lists of lower temporal whiskers, through cut $r-1$.

Take $e_x$ on each spatially moving cap variable to be the identity 2-cell on its identity change. For a pure $r$-term $v$ the collapse of $Dv$ is a loop $d_v:v\to v$. Take its pure comparison $p:d_v\to1_v$ to represent $e_v$. On either padded cap $W^e(v)$ the resulting contraction is, in the bicategory,
\[
             W^e(p)\cdot\bigl(W^e(1_v)\Rightarrow1_{W^e(v)}\bigr).
\]
For one lower whisker the top graft is literally the whisker on the moving operand, and its unit comparison is computed in the two-disk chart of that whisker. It can therefore be used as its pure $U$ in the preceding proof. Compose these formulas through the occupied cuts. All stationary proper-pole changes stay fixed. In particular at a change variable $H_x$ equation (1) holds by the disk formula itself: contract the padded minus identity against $H_x$ and expand the padded plus identity on its other side. This verifies the variable hypothesis in the actual mixed context.

Apply (1) to $f=H_v$ with its previously assigned raw syntax. Its spatial square $J_f$ is exactly the evaluation of Response 20's promoted temporal action when all strings collapse. Put $L:W^-(1_v)\ast H_v\Rightarrow H_v$ and $R:H_v\ast W^+(1_v)\Rightarrow H_v$ for the two disk contractions. With $F=W^-(p)\ast H_v$ and $B=H_v\ast W^+(p)$ the equation reads
\[
                         F\cdot L=J_f\cdot B\cdot R .
\]
The disk action $H_{1_v}$ is $L\cdot R^{-1}$ in this quotient. Each reversed unit comparison used by the raw disk formula is inverse to its forward comparison in its globular chart, and whiskering preserves this cancellation. Consequently there is an actual CaTT cell
\[
                        F\cdot H_{1_v}\longrightarrow J_f\cdot B .
\]
This is the ordinary action on the pure comparison $p$ at the two **specified** actions on $d_v$ and $1_v$. Its strict value is an identity by the height of $G$.

For a generic $k$-head on the original arity, first replace any grouped composites of identity blocks by a single identity per slot, using the recursive temporal-string result of Response 21 and its simultaneous top-slot replacement. The reference term now collapses to $b\ast d_v$, where $b:u\to v$ is the previously handled thin head on $S$. Use the comparison just constructed in the right input of this product, keeping the left input and its action fixed. This gives the comparison to $b\ast1_v$ with the recursive binary actions at its ends. The temporal right-unit law of Response 21 compares this to $b$ at its assigned action; it specializes from the one-arrow path with label $b$. When $u=v$ the reference is $d_v$ alone and its comparison goes directly to $1_v$. These are exactly the two normal writings on $S$. The pure cap adjustments used for the original generic head transport the comparison to that head by Response 19. This proves the complete-collapse test.

### Eliminating units before testing a substitution

In an elementary substitution $h[\sigma]$ suppose a top input is $1_z$. Its two formal positions have the *same raw label* $z$. Identify these positions and send the intervening top arrow to the weak identity. Then $\sigma$ factors through this pure degeneracy and a labelling of the shorter arity. The latter can have arbitrary lower labels. The corresponding temporal substitutions compose literally: the disk action on the formal identity specializes to the disk action on $1_z$ with the supplied $H_z$ and its own proper-pole changes. A missing change stays omitted by pullback of the marks.

The pure degeneracy has its elementary comparison by Response 21 when another top arrow survives, and by the complete-collapse result when this was the last one. Specialize that comparison along the remaining labelling. Both writings have the same literal type and strict value in the final arity, so their normal writings coincide. It suffices to continue with the shorter head. A variable gives an already normalized argument; a normal identity gives the identity on the substituted pole. Otherwise the shorter normal writing is again a single head, with the remaining top labels still normalized. Each step eliminates one formal top arrow. This also handles substitutions in which the marked slot belongs to a different branch.

One can further restrict the positive-height arities in the remaining tests to **binary temporal composition and trees having at most one top arrow per top string**. In a longer string first group all its top arrows into one block. The shortened tree has the same signed truncation, so give its generic head the original signed attaching terms. Its pure subdivision comparison on the original tree exists by Response 21. Specializing exhibits the old writing as comparable, at its assigned action, to the shortened head with the temporal products of the actual block labels. Normalize each product from the leaves of a binary bracketing. Each binary step uses the elementary binary test on its two normalized labels and the top-slot replacement on its arguments. The unbiased block writing compares to that bracketing by Response 21 with the actual input actions. Substitute these comparisons into the shortened head and apply its elementary test. Identity labels can again be eliminated as above. This is a sufficiency reduction: binary tests with arbitrary composable normalized labels remain, as do the stated one-arrow-per-string and lower-height tests with nonvariable lower labels. Neither the unrestricted globe-pair assertion nor Q1 follows yet.

**Verification.** The checker constructs the second depth-one substitution on two composable opposed squares, with both the full and a one-sided choice of moving poles. It calculates the substituted boundary in two ways. In another case the immediate pole is a composite and the second variation has a concrete witness on a twice-promoted globular tree. The complete collapse uses an exterior temporal mark and a nonvariable operation with a nonidentity change. The checker also factors a collapse through a disk labelled by that nonvariable term and compares both specialized identity changes literally. Agda checks the mixed substitutions and representative judgments. The separate disk paste takes the normalized equation as a hypothesis and builds the comparison at the raw disk faces, including cancellation of the specific reversed right correction. [Checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_spatial_units.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_spatial_units.py 4
python3 -B tools/gray_spatial_units.py 2 --paste --agda /tmp/GraySpatialUnitAll.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GraySpatialUnitAll.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 23

**Binary temporal composition passes the elementary substitution test at every rank of the induction in Response 21.** The two normalized inputs can meet at an arbitrary composite face. Even after collapsing the top strings, the source, middle and target faces can be three different raw terms. The result uses two facts about the promotion in Response 20. Splitting a spatial string preserves the promoted temporal action up to comparison, and the spatial square of a thin bridge has a temporal action at its prescribed faces.

Put $k=r+1$. Use $\ast$ for composition at cut $r$ and $\cdot$ for composition at cut $r+1$, both in order of traversal. Calculate in the bicategory of Response 21, whose 2-cells are $(r+2)$-terms modulo invertible $(r+3)$-comparison with fixed faces. Every equality used below in a labelled temporal cylinder of height at most $r+2$ yields an actual comparison there. In formulas with multiple products insert the string associators, and at an inactive position insert and cancel the temporary unitors of Response 21.

### The composition law for two spatial changes

Consider two successive spatial labellings $a,b$ and one direct labelling $c$ with the same outer caps. The first spatial marks have degree $r$. Write $D_z^a,D_z^b,D_z^c$ for the three changes on an $r$-term. On a marked $r$-variable give a comparison $m_x:D_x^c\Rightarrow D_x^a\ast D_x^b$. For an $(r+1)$-term $f:s\to t$, write $f_0,f_1,f_2$ for the three caps and $J_f$ for its spatial action opposed in degree $r+2$. Paste the two squares to obtain
\[
 P_f=(D_s^a\ast J_f^b)\cdot(J_f^a\ast D_t^b):
 (D_s^a\ast D_s^b)\ast f_2\Rightarrow f_0\ast(D_t^a\ast D_t^b).
\]
Suppose on each marked $(r+1)$-variable the following equation holds. Then it holds on every $(r+1)$-term, with the induced comparisons on its two poles:
\[
             (m_s\ast f_2)\cdot P_f=J_f^c\cdot(f_0\ast m_t).       \tag{2}
\]

To define $m_z$, first take an $r$-term on a pure globular arity. Graft two composable $(r+1)$-arrows at each active top slot. On this globular tree choose $M_z:D_z(a\ast b)\Rightarrow D_z(a)\ast D_z(b)$. Both faces evaluate strictly to the same cell. Indeed the strict $r$-diagram acts on a top change in each of its moving positions, and strict interchange separates the first changes from the second. Globular boundary lifting supplies $M_z$ and identifies the choices up to comparison. For arbitrary labels first vary the top change labels in $D_z$ along the supplied $m_x$, then use $M_z$. This prescription respects substitution. To check a composite substitution, split each inner $m$ into its variation and its $M$. The variation in a composite of two comparisons distributes by the same construction on the graft of two vertically composable $(r+2)$-arrows. The first variations now combine by ordinary substitution for depth-zero action, once in each top degree. Both remaining routes are comparisons on the pure two-step graft of the inner arity with the same literal endpoints. That graft has height at most $r+1$, and the routes have the same strict identity value, so they agree in the quotient before labelling. This gives the substitution law successively at every head of $z$.

For a pure arity $Q$ of height at most $r+1$, glue two opposed spatial cylinders of $Q$ along their common middle cap, with the same mask beginning in rank $r$. Call the result $W$. In its strict category any two parallel $(r+2)$-cells are equal. To see this, decompose a globular tree as a wedge of suspensions. Below rank $r$ the marks are stationary, so gluing commutes with each such suspension and wedge. The strict hom across consecutive branches is the product of the corresponding strict homs. Descend $r$ times, dropping trivial coordinates. At the last level the tree is a path. There are three copies of each selected horizontal edge and two vertical steps at each selected vertex. An opposed square in a column rewrites a source vertical followed by the upper horizontal to the lower horizontal followed by a target vertical, omitting an absent vertical. A path visits each horizontal position at most once. Its initial and final words therefore prescribe the moves in each column, in order of decreasing stage. Two enabled moves in different columns occupy disjoint subwords, since a move consumes only its own horizontal and possibly a vertical at its source. An enabled move stays enabled across all other moves until it fires. Thus the first move of one schedule can be commuted to the front of any other schedule with the same target. Induction on the length proves equality by strict exchange. This also treats isolated marks and columns with neither end selected. The $(r+1)$-skeleton of $W$ has variable faces and each new top square has variable $r$-poles. Consequently Response 13 gives a raw comparison between any two parallel $(r+2)$-terms in $W$.

Now induct on $f=h[\sigma]$. Use two labellings of the one-stage opposed cylinder of the head arity. The first evaluates the direct changes on the arguments of $\sigma$. The second first maps into $W$ by composing the two changes at each $r$-position and pasting the two squares at each $(r+1)$-position, then evaluates the stage labels on the arguments. Vary only the change variables of the one-stage cylinder between these labellings. At an $r$-position use $m_{\sigma x}$. At an $(r+1)$-position the declaration asks for (2) on the proper argument $\sigma x$: varying a single moving operand of either edge whisker is the higher whisker on its change. Choose the witness to the induction hypothesis there. The lowest variable varied in this second cylinder has rank $r+1$. Depth-one naturality on $J_h$ therefore compares the two evaluations, with exactly these variations at the poles. For the second evaluation, the direct square with the pure $M$ corrections and the pasted stage squares already agree in $W$ by the preceding paragraph. Specialize and compose. The substitution law for $m$ identifies the two outer corrections with those at the actual poles of $f$. This proves (2), including when $f$ is a previously chosen term on a mixed context. The substitution and depth-one operations used here are those of [Benjamin–Markakis–Offord–Sarti–Vicary, Theorem 3.1 and Lemma 3.3](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.11620).

Apply (2) to $f=H_w$ on $S\uparrow Y$ in the notation of Response 20. Take $a,b$ to be the two portions of each spatial string and $c$ the full string. On the two temporal copies of a spatial slot, $m_x$ is the pure comparison from the full string to the product of the portions. At the change variable $H_x$, (2) is exactly temporal regrouping of that string at the recursive actions, proved in Response 21, with the lower cap distributors. Indeed for a fixed lower whisker $F$ the pole comparison $m_{Fz}$ is $F(m_z)$ followed by the distributor $F(D^a_z\ast D^b_z)\Rightarrow F(D^a_z)\ast F(D^b_z)$. At one whisker this is an allowed choice on its pure two-step graft, hence agrees with $M$ there. Iterate over the occupied cuts. The same calculation identifies (2) on $H_w$ as a temporal action on the pure comparison
\[
                    D^c w\longrightarrow D^a w\ast D^b w
\]
at the promoted action on the left and the recursive binary action on the right. Empty portions use disk actions and the unit law of Response 22. The reverse pure comparison also has an action by Response 21.

### Moving a thin bridge across a spatial change

Let $b:u\to v$ be a pure $(r+1)$-term over an arity $S$ of height at most $r$, with temporal action $K:p\ast h_v\Rightarrow h_u\ast q$. Here $p,q$ are the minus and plus copies of $b$ with their proper temporal whiskers below $r$, and $h_u=H_u$, $h_v=H_v$. Write $A=p\ast h_v$, $B=h_u\ast q$. Make a single spatial change of the top slots of $S$, with its label and temporal change as in Response 20. The pure opposed square is
\[
                         j_b:Du\ast b_1\Rightarrow b_0\ast Dv.
\]
It admits a temporal action at the recursively computed actions on these two products.

For the orientation, apply ordinary depth-two spatial naturality to $\mathrm{op}_{r+2}K:B\Rightarrow A$, then oppose degree $r+2$ in the resulting judgment. The old context has height at most $r+1$ and is fixed by this opposition. Use the depth-two extension of Response 13 with its ordinary depth-zero and depth-one boundary actions. Before opposition, the source is the product of the whiskered initial copy of $\mathrm{op}_{r+2}K$ with the ordinary square on $A$. The target is the ordinary square on $B$ followed by the whiskered final copy. Opposition reverses products at cut $r+1$ and returns $K$ on both copies. Thus the new cell has the following faces, where $e,f$ are the changes on the padded minus poles of $u,v$ and $e',f'$ those on the plus poles:
\[
              J_A\cdot(K_0\ast f')\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
                         (e\ast K_1)\cdot J_B.                      \tag{3}
\]
This applies to any raw $K$, by the substitution recursion for depth two. Its two caps are precisely the given instances of $K$.

For clarity, name the four other squares
\[
 P:e\ast p_1\Rightarrow p_0\ast f,\quad
 Q:e'\ast q_1\Rightarrow q_0\ast f',\quad
 U:e\ast h_{u,1}\Rightarrow h_{u,0}\ast e',\quad
 V:f\ast h_{v,1}\Rightarrow h_{v,0}\ast f'.
\]
Here $U,V$ are the promoted actions on $Du,Dv$. The opposed square of a binary path agrees with the paste of its operand squares in path order: both have the same faces on the generic one-band path cylinder, so the strip argument and Response 13 compare them before substitution. Likewise the opposed square of a fixed lower whisker on $b$ agrees with the whisker of $j_b$ after distributing the two products at its ends. Apply the same argument on the one-band cylinder of the pure whisker arity, holding the lower operand stationary, and then substitute. This identifies $P,Q$ with the two padded copies of $j_b$, including the distributors, through any number of occupied cuts.

Expand the source of (3) in order as $P\ast h_{v,1}$, $p_0\ast V$, $K_0\ast f'$. The last two factors give the temporal action on $b_0\ast Dv$. Expand the target as $e\ast K_1$, $U\ast q_1$, $h_{u,0}\ast Q$. Its first two factors give the temporal action on $Du\ast b_1$. The cap distributors adjacent to these two pastes cancel their inverses in the expressions for $P,Q$ by the pseudofunctor laws of Response 21. Rebracket at both exterior ends. These are exactly the two faces for the action on $j_b$. All adjustments lift to raw comparisons. In particular (3) never requires an inverse for $H_u$ or $H_v$.

### Choosing the bridge actions and folding two arbitrary factors

At the preceding stage, the $k$-actions on thin heads can be chosen compatibly with products of bridges on the same pure arity. Fix such an arity, a full literal type of rank $r$ terms on it, and their common strict value. Let $n$ be the normal writing. Choose a pure equivalence $l_u:u\to n$ for each writing $u$ in this class and put $l_n=1_n$. The preceding stage supplies an invertible square $L_u:F(l_u)H_n\Rightarrow H_u G(l_u)$, where $F,G$ are the two padded caps. Invertibility holds because $L_u$ has degree $r+2$ over a cylinder of height at most $r+1$. Use the disk square at $n$. For any pure bridge $b:u\to v$, there is a unique pure 2-cell class $\theta_b:b\ast l_v\Rightarrow l_u$ by globular boundary lifting. Choose the square $K_b$ by the equation
\[
 (F\theta_b\ast H_n)\cdot L_u
  =(Fb\ast L_v)\cdot(K_b\ast G l_v)\cdot(H_u\ast G\theta_b)
\]
with the two cap compositors inserted. This is solvable uniquely in the quotient: cancel the invertible outer 2-cells, then right whiskering by the equivalence $G l_v$ is fully faithful, with inverse given by whiskering by its inverse and using its unit and counit. This defines an invertible square and gives the disk square for an identity. For two bridges $b:u\to v$, $d:v\to w$, append $l_w$ to their pasted square. Applying the defining equation for $d$ and then for $b$ gives the defining equation for a direct bridge $u\to w$, transported along the pure comparison out of $b\ast d$. The two resulting pure triangles agree by uniqueness of the pure 2-cell class. Faithfulness after whiskering by $G l_w$ gives the desired comparison of squares. Install representatives on generic thin heads on their own supports, and use their variable inclusions. This construction uses the freedom to choose those heads before constructing the positive-height heads at rank $k$. It makes no assumption about the induced action on an arbitrary substitution into a thin head.

Take normalized composable $k$-terms on the support $P$ of their product. If $P$ has height below $k$, both strict values are identities on the same cell, so each factor already lives on all of $P$. They are generic bridges or disk identities, and the preceding construction gives the elementary comparison. At shallow selections the required $(k+1)$-comparison actions can also be lifted directly by Response 13 whenever their temporal depth is at most two.

Otherwise $P$ has height $k$. Its common $r$-truncation is $S$. The strict factorization picks a middle position in every top string. Indeed, decomposing by wedge and suspension down to rank $r$ takes the cut-$r$ product coordinatewise. In each remaining path, factorization of a path is a cut at a vertex, including either endpoint. The supports of the factors are exactly these two portions together with the unchanged branches. Their three signed sections therefore identify with three inclusions $i_0,i_1,i_2$ of $S$. Pull the actual pole terms back to them, giving $u_0,u_1,u_2$ with common literal type and strict value. In particular the middle writing is shared literally by the two factors. For deeper threshold selections the marks are constant along each string, as in Response 20.

Choose generic bridges $b_{ij}:u_i\to u_j$, using the identity for equal writings. By Responses 20–22, the two factors and the direct normal writing have respectively the following reference terms with their assigned actions, up to pure cap corrections:
\[
 b_{01}[i_0]\ast D^a u_1,\qquad b_{12}[i_1]\ast D^b u_2,
 \qquad b_{02}[i_0]\ast D^c u_2 .
\]
For equal writings insert the identity bridge by the temporal unit law. For an entirely empty portion use the complete-collapse comparison. A variable on a disk retains its declared action. Change the two product factors to their references by top-slot replacement. Reassociate and apply the crossing just proved to $D^a u_1\ast b_{12}[i_1]$. This moves the second bridge to $i_0$ and changes the following factor to $D^a u_2$. Now combine the two bridges on $i_0$ using the compatible choice above, and combine $D^a u_2\ast D^b u_2$ to $D^c u_2$ using (2) in reverse. Every step has an action at its stated endpoints and can be used inside the surrounding product by Response 21. Finally change the direct reference to the normal writing using the cap corrections of Response 19. This constructs the comparison for the binary fold. The remaining elementary substitutions of Response 22 can consequently be restricted to lower-height heads and to positive-height heads with at most one arrow per top string and nonvariable lower labels.

**Verification.** For a width-three strip with three bands the checker enumerated all 34 masks and 4,956 paths. Each of the 12,016 source–target cases with several schedules was connected by swaps of independent moves. It built a typed comparison between both ways of pasting four positive squares. It also realized the pure fold on two slots in degrees two through four with three distinct pole writings, including a wholly empty first portion and a wholly empty support in top degree. For (3) it used the oriented cube face as a hypothesis, constructed both bracket transfers in CaTT, used the cube once, and checked the resulting raw term at the independent temporal faces in Agda safe mode. The certificate checks this transfer. The existence of the depth-two face and the arbitrary-rank fold are supplied by the proofs above. [Checker and Agda certificate generator](../../../tools/gray_spatial_composition.py)

~~~sh
python3 -B tools/gray_spatial_composition.py --width 3 --stages 3 --agda /tmp/GrayBridgeCube.agda
agda --safe --no-libraries -i /tmp -i src /tmp/GrayBridgeCube.agda
~~~

# Researcher Response 24

**At rank $k$, it suffices to run the elementary substitution test on targets of height strictly less than $k$.** In particular, whenever the target has height $k$, the test reduces to one on its signed boundary of height $k-1$. The reduction keeps the source head. Its new top inputs are normal bridges between the two boundary labellings. This covers nonvariable lower labels, including an input of degree $k-1$ supported entirely in smaller degree. As in Response 21 this is a criterion at one stage of the induction, with the preceding stages available.

### Omitting changes at stationary positions

Here is the unit calculation needed when substitution changes which positions move. Work spatially through rank $r+1$, and let $L\subset M$ be two up-closed selections starting in rank at least $r$. Label the opposed $L$-cylinder. To extend the labelling to $M$, give each extra $r$-position the identity on its common cap. For an $r$-term $z$ the extended change $D_z^M$ admits a comparison $e_z$ to $D_z^L$, using an identity on $z$ when the latter change is absent. Use this comparison in the following calculation only where the larger mask has a factor. When a smaller-mask factor is absent, contract its temporary identity by its unitor.

At an $(r+1)$-variable $y:s\to t$, give the larger mask the smaller square with $e_s$ on its incoming side and $e_t^{-1}$ on its outgoing side. If $y$ itself is stationary for $L$, use the identity square on its common cap with these same exterior corrections. More generally the given larger square may agree with this formula modulo comparison. Then **the same formula holds for every $(r+1)$-term** at the two evaluated labellings. In particular the comparison concerns the already chosen spatial action of the term.

To prove this first take an $r$-term on its pure arity, pull both masks back, and graft a single change at each smaller-mask top slot. Evaluate the larger change using these changes and identities elsewhere. Its comparison to the smaller change, or to the identity when all slots are stationary, exists on this pure graft. The cells have the same strict value by insertion of strict identities. Parallel choices agree up to comparison since the graft has height at most $r+1$. To extend $e$ from variables with given comparisons to terms, first vary the change labels in $D^M$ along their comparisons, then apply this pure comparison. For a composite substitution the first variations compose by depth-zero substitution in the two successive degrees. The remaining routes are comparisons with the same endpoints on the smaller-mask pure graft of the inner arity, where they agree. Varying a composite comparison is justified on the graft with a pair of composable comparison cells at each changing leaf as in Response 22. This gives the substitution law for $e_z$, including when $z$ has no surviving change.

For $f=h[\rho]$ compare two labellings of the larger opposed cylinder on the arity of $h$. The first uses the larger evaluated data on the proper arguments. For the second map that cylinder into the smaller one by giving the extra top positions identities and inserting the stated pole corrections at its square variables, then specialize. Vary only the change variables between these two labellings. Their lowest rank is $r+1$. At each change of an $r$-argument use its $e$. At each square use the hypothesis for the proper $(r+1)$-argument, with exterior whiskers on its two $e$'s. This is exactly the ordinary change declaration because each variable changed in that whisker is its top operand. Now apply depth-one naturality to the larger $J_h$. For the second labelling its corrected comparison to the smaller $J_h$ holds in the generic one-band smaller cylinder: the two terms are parallel after the indicated whiskerings and unitors, and the strip proof of Response 23 and Response 13 supply a comparison. When $h$ is stationary in the smaller cylinder use the identity on its single cap. Substitute, then use the substitution law for the two $e$'s. This gives the assertion by induction, with inverse adjustments witnessed in degrees above the height of the relevant temporal context. Only the pole comparisons $e$ need inverses in the displayed square. They are invertible because the initial ones are units or equivalences and the graft comparisons are above the height of the pure graft.

### Restricting a substitution to its two signed sections

By Responses 22 and 23 take a generic $k$-head $h$ on $Q$ with at most one arrow per top string, evaluated by $\sigma$ on its full support $P$ of height $k$. A head on a smaller arity cannot have a positive $k$-cell as its value after substitution: its strict value is an identity. Write $r=k-1$, and $R,S$ for the common signed $r$-truncations of $Q,P$. Write $i_0,i_1$ for the inclusions of $S$ in $P$. The shallow threshold cases follow from Response 13, so take the threshold below $r$. Its selection is constant along each top string.

Restrict $\sigma$ to each signed section of $Q$. This yields two labellings $\tau_0,\tau_1$ of $R$ in $S$. Indeed, every argument on that section occurs in the typed support of the corresponding pole of $h$. After substitution this pole has the corresponding signed support of $P$, so all those arguments factor through that variable inclusion. The two restrictions agree literally below rank $r$ since the sections coincide there. For a top slot $x$ of $R$, the two $r$-terms $\tau_0x,\tau_1x$ are parallel and have the same strict value. In a slot carrying an arrow, project its label strictly from $P$ to $S$ by contracting every top string: it becomes the identity on this value. In a slot without an arrow both raw labels are already equal. Let $b_x$ be the normal pure bridge in $S$ from $\tau_0x$ to $\tau_1x$, on their common support, with its assigned temporal action if active. Use the identity for equal terms. These labels and the two sections give a substitution $\sigma_0$ of the *same* $Q$ in $S$. Every actual degree-$k$ entry of $\sigma_0$ is normalized. The pulled-back temporal selection on $Q$ is the same for the two substitutions: contracting a top string preserves its proper threshold positions, and the bridge retains their full typed support.

In $S$ formally move every top slot as in Response 21. Evaluate its change in $P$ by the full string at that slot, or by the disk identity when the string is empty. Denote this operation by $D$. For any slot $x$ let $d_x=D(\tau_1x)$, using the identity if its support contains no $r$-slot. In the latter case both spatial copies of $\tau_1x$ are the same raw term. Give $d_x$ the promoted action, or the temporal disk action in the stationary case. The original top input $f_x$ is comparable, with its prescribed action, to
\[
                           b_x[i_0]\ast d_x.                         \tag{4}
\]
For this assertion use the support subarity of $f_x$. Its source and target use precisely the supports of $\tau_0x$ and $\tau_1x$ on the two signed sections. In the path coordinates at rank $r$, an interval between these two sections traverses the entire string at every slot belonging to this common support. To see this decompose the tree by wedge and suspension through its first $r$ levels. Composition in a strict hom across branches is coordinatewise. In each remaining path the two endpoints are the first and last position, fixing the interval even when it has length zero. Consequently the local template for the normal $f_x$ is the restriction of the full-string template used in $D$ to this subarity. Its reference is exactly (4), with its specified action by Responses 20–21. Use the cap corrections there, inserting a unit bridge for equal poles. If all local strings vanish, use Response 22. If the support even has height below $r$, then $f_x=b_x[i_0]$ by normalization and (4) is just a right unit. A stationary slot of $Q$ has a disk identity for its formal $f_x$ and all its local strings are empty, giving the same comparison. This supplies (4) at every slot, including one disjoint from the temporal marks.

### Splitting the reference action

Let $u,v$ be the signed pole writings of $h$ on $R$, and $b:u\to v$ its chosen pure bridge. Insert the identity bridge if the writings coincide. The Response 21 reference for $h[\sigma]$ first uses $b[\tau_0][i_0]$ and then the promoted action on $v$ evaluated at the $f_x$. Apply the composition law (2) of Response 23 to $H_v$. Its first spatial stage uses the $b_x$ on the initial section. The second stage uses the $d_x$ starting at $\tau_1[i_0]$ and ending at $\tau_1[i_1]$. At a temporal cap variable the compositor is the pure comparison (4) in that cap. At a change variable $H_x$ the required equation is precisely the temporal action on (4), including the lower whisker distributors as in (2). Group the resulting three factors as
\[
             \bigl(b[\tau_0]\ast D^a v\bigr)[i_0]
